===== codex-docs/FIX-154_TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md =====
# FIX-154 D"?" Trusted higher-timeframe BUY validation

Status: implementation design approved. FIX-154 is scheduled before the
remaining FIX-153 runtime release work.

## Purpose and boundary

FIX-154 replaces the current binary fresh-1h bearish veto with a trusted
higher-timeframe assessment. It does not relax the existing 5m confirmation
requirement, risk/reward result, current-1m BTC protection, or profile rules
except where a raw 1h decision would otherwise act as an untrusted veto.

The legacy `src/main/resources/md/FIX-146.md` is an unrelated shared-market
readiness design and is not an implementation source for this fix.

## Current integration points to replace

The 1h/5m blocks in all three paths below must be replaced as a whole by one
shared assessment call. Do not add this decision to `FinalDecisionService`.

1. `TradeExecutionValidationService.validateBuy()` D"?" direct 1m BUYs.
2. `TradeExecutionValidationService.validateBuyContext()` D"?" accumulated/setup
   authority, whose reference may be a 5m signal.
3. `OpportunityConsolidationService.evaluate()` D"?" consolidated BUYs.

Each replacement owns the current lookup, freshness, 5m veto, and 1h veto
block in that method. The remaining profile/consolidation logic consumes the
assessment's effective context; it must not reread the raw 1h decision as an
independent veto.

## Immutable signal evidence

Migration `V107` adds:

```sql
ALTER TABLE trade_signal ADD COLUMN data_quality_valid BOOLEAN NULL;
```

`AnalysisService` writes `MarketContextSnapshot.dataValid()` through the
existing `TradeSignal.builder()` call for every newly generated signal on every
interval. Historic rows are never backfilled: `NULL` means unknown/invalid.

FIX-154 does not depend on `signal_mode` or any other FIX-153 runtime/schema
feature. `data_quality_valid = true` is sufficient evidence eligibility for
this release; false or `NULL` is unusable. Historical-only exclusion is added
later with FIX-153, after its own migration/runtime release is approved.

Historical `btcContextEntryAllowed=false` is audit evidence only. It is never a
FIX-154 usability or hard-veto predicate. The current 1m BTC guard is unchanged.

## Time-bounded selection and fixed freshness

For a request with reference signal `R`, use `R.generatedAt` as `evaluatedAt`.
Select the latest same-symbol 5m and 1h signal such that both its generated
time and its candle close are at or before `evaluatedAt`; no wall-clock lookup
is permitted. Replay supplies the same historical reference time.

Existing fixed freshness limits remain authoritative:

| Interval | Maximum age |
|---|---:|
| 5m | 20 minutes |
| 1h | 3 hours |

`MISSING_5M_CONFIRMATION` and `MISSING_1H_CONTEXT` retain their existing
meaning and priority when their respective interval is missing or stale. A
stale signal is not quietly converted into an allowed BUY just because it is
`NOT_TRUSTED` for FIX-154.

## Usability and 1h strength

A fresh row is usable for FIX-154 only if `data_quality_valid = true`.

FIX-154 v1 does not invent ADX/DMI/VWAP/CMF evidence, because those indicators
are not currently persisted. Its fixed strength definition is:

```text
HARD_BEARISH_CANDIDATE = 1h decision == STRONG_SELL
                         AND confidence_score >= 70
SOFT_CONFLICT          = fresh, quality-valid 1h decision == SELL
                         OR STRONG_SELL with confidence_score below 70
NOT_TRUSTED            = fresh but quality-invalid/unknown/historical-only 1h
                         OR fresh non-bearish 1h
```

`confidence_score` is the persisted effective confidence. `raw_confidence_score`
is explicitly not used. The constant `70` is a named FIX-154 configuration
default, persisted in the audit row, and may change only through a reviewed
strategy change with Replay regression.

## Exact 1h A- 5m decision matrix

First apply existing missing/stale checks. Only then apply this table.

| 1h state | 5m state | Final HTF result before profile | Profile inputs |
|---|---|---|---|
| missing/stale | any | existing `MISSING_1H_CONTEXT` rejection | none |
| any | missing/stale | existing `MISSING_5M_CONFIRMATION` rejection | none |
| NOT_TRUSTED bearish from false/NULL quality | bearish | legacy `5M_BEARISH_VETO` | none |
| NOT_TRUSTED bearish from false/NULL quality | bullish/neutral/watch | `1H_BEARISH_VETO` | none |
| NOT_TRUSTED non-bearish from false/NULL quality | bearish | legacy `5M_BEARISH_VETO` | none |
| NOT_TRUSTED non-bearish from false/NULL quality | bullish/neutral/watch | continue to profile | raw 1h, raw 5m |
| SOFT_CONFLICT | bearish | legacy `5M_BEARISH_VETO` | none |
| SOFT_CONFLICT | bullish/neutral/watch | continue to profile, cap resulting first entry at 25% | effective 1h=`NEUTRAL`, raw 5m |
| HARD_BEARISH_CANDIDATE | bearish, quality-valid | legacy `5M_BEARISH_VETO`; audit `HARD_CONFIRMATION_PRESENT` | none |
| HARD_BEARISH_CANDIDATE | bullish/neutral/watch, quality-valid | `HTF_TRUSTED_BEARISH_VETO` | none |
| fresh, quality-valid non-bearish 1h | non-bearish | continue to profile | raw 1h, raw 5m |

The profile call produces the final decision for every `continue to profile`
row. It may still return its existing insufficient-confirmation code. It must
not consult the raw 1h row after this mapping.

`5m quality=false/NULL` does not alter the legacy 5m fallback. A fresh raw
bearish 5m still returns `5M_BEARISH_VETO`, exactly as today. It merely cannot
act as new FIX-154 hard-veto confirmation or a soft-conflict release.

The veto order is fixed: a bearish 5m returns `5M_BEARISH_VETO` before any
trusted-1h result. If the 1h is hard, audit records `HARD_CONFIRMATION_PRESENT`
on that legacy outcome. When 5m is not bearish, a hard trusted 1h returns
`HTF_TRUSTED_BEARISH_VETO`. The only deliberate lasting relaxation is a trusted
SOFT bearish 1h: an otherwise allowed initial entry is capped at 25%.

## Profile contract

`conservativeBuy`, `balancedBuy`, `balancedBuyWithNeutralFiveException`, and
`aggressiveBuy` receive the assessment's effective 1h/5m contextD"?"not raw
selected 1h evidence. A SOFT conflict maps to `NEUTRAL`, never `WATCH`, then
the validation result is capped with `min(profilePositionPercent, 25)`.
This means the neutral-5m/direct-BUY exception still requires a genuinely
bullish 1h and cannot be unlocked by SOFT evidence. An untrusted non-bearish
1h retains its raw decision so deployment does not accidentally tighten current
bullish authority.

The assessment result carries `maxInitialPercent`: `25` for `SOFT_CONFLICT`,
otherwise no cap. Every caller that selects a size must use
`min(itsCalculatedPercent, maxInitialPercent)` when the cap is present. This
includes direct validation, `validateBuyContext()` callers (the 5m-trigger
path and accumulated evidence), and
`OpportunityConsolidationService.consolidatedPositionPercent()`.

The raw selected signals remain audit evidence. Existing 5m requirements inside
profiles remain unchanged. HARD and untrusted-bearish outcomes never call a
profile method.

### Add-on contract

`ExecutionIntelligenceService.progressivePositionDecision()` already reads raw
1h evidence and requires `WATCH` or bullish support for `SCOUT_ENTRY`,
`CONFIRMATION_ADD`, and `PROGRESSIVE_TREND_ADD`. Keep that check unchanged and
outside the three replacement paths; it already blocks HARD, SOFT, and
untrusted-bearish 1h states. Do not route it through effective FIX-154 context,
which could widen behavior. When that existing check rejects while the assessed
band is SOFT, record `SOFT_HTF_ADD_BLOCKED` as audit/metric evidence only.

## Append-only execution audit

FIX-154 is evaluated during execution/consolidation, not while a signal is
saved. Migration `V107` creates append-only `buy_htf_assessment_audit`:

- primary key ID;
- reference signal ID, evaluation path (`DIRECT_BUY`, `BUY_CONTEXT`,
  `CONSOLIDATION`, `PROGRESSIVE_TREND_ADD`, `CONFIRMATION_ADD`,
  `ACCUMULATED_EVIDENCE_ADD`) and `evaluated_at`; no unique constraint;
- raw/effective 1h and 5m signal IDs, decisions, signal modes, candle closes,
  ages, data-quality values, and usability reasons;
- configured confidence threshold, band, legacy-veto result, profile context,
  final result/reason, Production/Replay mode, and creation timestamp.

The audit is written best-effort after the relevant execution/consolidation
decision is known. A duplicate or persistence failure is logged and increments
`fix154.audit_write_failure_count`; it must never roll back, reject, or alter a
trading decision. It is evidence onlyD"?"not a fence or an input to later trading.

## Deployment behavior

Immediately after deployment all pre-V107 1h/5m rows have
`data_quality_valid=NULL`. They are treated as `NOT_TRUSTED`; they cannot make a
new permissive decision: a bearish unknown 1h remains blocked, while a
non-bearish unknown 1h retains its existing profile authority. The health view
must expose `fix154.untrusted_bearish_1h_block_count` and
`fix154.audit_write_failure_count`, `fix154.soft_capped_entry_count`, and
`fix154.soft_add_blocked_count`, grouped by execution path.

## Migration ordering and FIX-153 independence

The configured local database has V106 as its latest applied Flyway migration.
Therefore FIX-154 reserves `V107__fix_154_trusted_higher_timeframe_buy.sql`.
Before this migration is applied anywhere, rename the currently unreleased
`V107__fix_153_local_candle_reconciliation.sql` to V108. Never rename or edit
an applied migration. Do not enable Flyway `outOfOrder`; deployment order is
made explicit instead.

## Required regression suite

- All three integration paths use the shared service and no longer contain raw
  `1H_BEARISH_VETO` branches.
- `STRONG_SELL` + `confidence_score >= 70` and bearish fresh valid 5m produces
  legacy `5M_BEARISH_VETO` with `HARD_CONFIRMATION_PRESENT` audit; the same 1h
  with non-bearish 5m produces `HTF_TRUSTED_BEARISH_VETO`.
- Strong/soft/untrusted 1h plus bullish, neutral, watch, bearish, missing,
  stale, false-quality, and NULL-quality 5m covers every matrix row and its
  final profile result.
- `MISSING_1H_CONTEXT` (3h) and `MISSING_5M_CONFIRMATION` (20m) retain their
  priority and codes.
- BNB #150a: persisted historical 1h bearish row with `NULL` quality =>
  `NOT_TRUSTED` and `1H_BEARISH_VETO`.
- BNB #150b: Replay regenerates that same 1h with quality evidence and it is
  SOFT; no trusted hard veto is emitted and the final expected result is
  `REJECTED_RISK_REWARD` when the risk/reward guard fails.
- Historical `btcContextEntryAllowed=false` does not change usability; current
  1m BTC behavior is unchanged.
- FIX-154 migration succeeds with no `signal_mode` column and its assessment
  has no FIX-153 schema/runtime dependency.
- No future signal/candle is selected in Production or Replay; both produce
  equivalent audit evidence for the same historical inputs.
- Pre-migration `NULL` quality is never treated as valid.
- Repeated execution/consolidation evaluations append audit rows and audit
  failure leaves the business decision intact.
- A SOFT initial entry never exceeds 25% through progressive, confirmation, or
  accumulated-evidence or consolidation sizing; existing progressive raw-1h
  support rules block add stages while the 1h remains bearish.

## Release conditions

Run Java 21 unit/integration tests, migration tests, Production/Replay parity,
and a bounded no-look-ahead historical replay before enabling FIX-154.
FIX-153 remains independent and retains its own release gates.

## Revision addendum D"?" Round 5 approval conditions

This addendum governs where it differs from earlier wording.

### Release build decision

The FIX-154 release build includes `V108__fix_153_local_candle_reconciliation.sql`
after the current unreleased FIX-153 V107 migration is renumbered to V108.
This is required even with reconciliation disabled: `candle_analysis_outcome`
is used by ordinary live signal processing, terminal worker outcomes, and the
closed-candle detector. Omitting V108 would cause missing-table SQL failures;
in particular, a normal signal-processing completion would roll back because
its outcome write is inside the processing transaction.

Including V108 ships FIX-153 reconciliation *schema only*. The reconciliation
runtime remains disabled and retains all of its separate release gates.

### No FIX-153 provenance dependency

Ignore earlier references to historical-only status or signal modes in the
FIX-154 band definition and audit payload. FIX-154 v1 eligibility depends only
on time-bounded selection and `data_quality_valid = true`; `signal_mode` is not
read, required, or stored by the FIX-154 audit.

### Effective context is transient

The assessment returns immutable effective decision values separately from the
loaded `TradeSignal` entities. No code may call `setDecision`, mutate, merge,
or save a selected 1h/5m signal to obtain effective `NEUTRAL` context. Profile
methods receive explicit effective decisions (or an immutable assessment
context), while the raw entity remains audit evidence only. A regression test
must load a SOFT 1h signal, execute the assessment/profile path, flush/clear,
and prove its stored decision is unchanged.

### Progressive-path evidence is observational

`progressivePositionDecision()` retains its existing raw-1h support check as
the only decision authority for scout/add paths. If that existing check passes,
FIX-154 performs no assessment lookup and writes no progressive audit row. If
it rejects because raw 1h is bearish, a best-effort audit-only assessment may
record `SOFT_HTF_ADD_BLOCKED` when the band is SOFT. That observation must not
alter the rejection, allow an add, cause a database lookup on passing paths, or
propagate an audit failure into the trading decision.

V108 ships with FIX-154 because the current runtime writes
`candle_analysis_outcome`. Reconciliation remains disabled.

### Round 5 text clarification

This addendum removes any earlier contrary implication: FIX-154 v1 has no
`signal_mode` or historical-only predicate or audit field. Effective 1h/5m
decisions are separate immutable values and never mutate a loaded signal.
Progressive-path assessment is audit-only after its existing raw-1h check has
already rejected; it is skipped entirely when that check passes and never feeds
the scout/add decision.

## Implementation completion record D"?" 2026-10-10

The approved FIX-154 design is now implemented in the working tree.

- `V107__fix_154_trusted_higher_timeframe_buy.sql` creates the nullable signal
  quality evidence and append-only assessment audit table. No historical value
  is fabricated or backfilled.
- `AnalysisService` persists `MarketContextSnapshot.dataValid()` for every new
  signal.
- `HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentService` owns the hard/soft/untrusted decision
  matrix. It returns immutable effective decisions and does not mutate a JPA
  signal.
- Direct BUY validation, accumulated-evidence authority, and opportunity
  consolidation invoke that one service. A SOFT result uses effective 1h
  `NEUTRAL` and applies the 25% initial-position cap. The legacy bearish-5m
  veto retains precedence. Progressive adds retain their existing raw 1h
  requirement and can only create best-effort audit evidence after rejection.
- `BuyHtfAssessmentAuditStore` is append-only and runs in an independent
  transaction. It catches insert failures so audit availability cannot alter a
  business decision.

Focused Java 21 verification passed:
`HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentServiceTest`,
`TradeExecutionValidationServiceTest`, and
`OpportunityConsolidationServiceTest`.

This is implementation completion, not production deployment approval. Apply
V107/V108 only through the normal clean Java 21 release pipeline and complete
the required migration, Replay-parity, and broader regression validation before
enabling it in Production.

===== codex-docs/FIX-154_IMPLEMENTATION_HANDOFF.md =====
# FIX-154 D"?" Implementation Handoff for Code Review

Date: 2026-10-10  
Status: implemented in the local working tree; not deployed.

## Scope

FIX-154 changes only higher-timeframe BUY authority. It does not enable or
change FIX-153 reconciliation/runtime behavior.

The objective is to distinguish trusted, strong bearish 1h evidence from
unknown or weaker evidence without changing a stored historical signal.

## Implemented policy

1. The current 1m BUY still requires fresh 5m and 1h context.
2. A bearish 5m returns the existing `5M_BEARISH_VETO` first.
3. A bearish 1h with `data_quality_valid` `NULL` or `false` returns
   `1H_BEARISH_VETO`.
4. A trusted 1h `STRONG_SELL` with `confidence_score >= 70` returns
   `HTF_TRUSTED_BEARISH_VETO`.
5. A trusted 1h `SELL`, or `STRONG_SELL` with confidence below 70, is a
   SOFT conflict. It passes effective 1h `NEUTRAL` to profile logic and caps
   every allowed initial entry at 25%.
6. A non-bearish 1h with unknown quality keeps its raw decision; FIX-154 does
   not tighten bullish/non-bearish authority merely because historical quality
   is absent.
7. Existing progressive/add logic retains its raw 1h requirement. A SOFT
   conflict cannot authorize an add. Optional `SOFT_HTF_ADD_BLOCKED` evidence
   is observational only.

## Database changes

`V107__fix_154_trusted_higher_timeframe_buy.sql` adds:

- `trade_signal.data_quality_valid BOOLEAN NULL`.
- Replay parity columns on `trade_signal_test`:
  `btc_context_candle_close_time`, `btc_context_age_seconds`,
  `btc_context_freshness_threshold_seconds`, `signal_mode`, and
  `data_quality_valid`.
- Append-only `buy_htf_assessment_audit`, including effective/raw decisions,
  candle-close-based age, quality evidence, the hard-confirmation marker,
  cap, legacy veto and final result.

Historical `data_quality_valid` stays `NULL`; no value is backfilled.

## Included production code

| File | Change |
|---|---|
| `domain/TradeSignal.java` | Maps nullable `data_quality_valid`. |
| `service/AnalysisService.java` | Persists `MarketContextSnapshot.dataValid()` at signal generation. |
| `service/HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentService.java` | Pure FIX-154 decision matrix; returns detached effective decisions and never mutates entities. |
| `service/TradeExecutionValidationService.java` | Applies assessment to direct BUY and contextual/accumulated authority; enforces SOFT cap; records non-blocking audit evidence. |
| `service/OpportunityConsolidationService.java` | Applies the same matrix and cap to consolidation. |
| `execution/service/ExecutionIntelligenceService.java` | Keeps existing progressive raw-1h guard; invokes optional audit-only SOFT evidence after its rejection. Uses `SETUP_WAKEUP` audit path for the 5m wake-up caller. |
| `service/BuyHtfAssessmentAuditStore.java` | Snapshots and asynchronously persists best-effort Production audit rows. Replay writes no Production audit row. |
| `config/Fix154AuditAsyncConfig.java` | Dedicated one-thread, bounded executor; audit saturation drops evidence rather than borrowing a trading connection. |
| `service/Fix154Metrics.java` | Non-gating counters for untrusted 1h blocks, audit failures, SOFT caps and SOFT add blocks. |
| `service/Fix154DiagnosticsController.java` | Read-only `GET /api/fix154/health` metrics endpoint. |
| `service/Fix154RuntimeSchemaChecks.java` | Checks V107-required production and Replay schema before runtime admission. |

## Replay behavior

The regular reflection-based Replay writer persists every JPA `TradeSignal`
column. V107 therefore updates `trade_signal_test` alongside production.
FIX-154 audit rows are deliberately skipped while `ExecutionReplayScope` is
active because replay-generated entities do not have Production IDs and parity
is recorded by ReplayD"?Ts own evidence tables.

## Tests included

- `HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentServiceTest`
  - hard, soft, untrusted, 5m-veto precedence, 69/70 boundary, BNB #150a and
    #150b policy cases, and unknown non-bearish authority.
- `TradeExecutionValidationServiceTest`
  - direct/context SOFT caps, conservative behavior, neutral-5m exception is
    not unlocked, audit failure isolation, Production/Replay parity, and
    future-context exclusion.
- `OpportunityConsolidationServiceTest`
  - SOFT cap through consolidation.
- `BuyHtfAssessmentAuditStoreTest`
  - Replay does not write a Production audit row.
- `Fix154MigrationContractTest`
  - V107 contains production, Replay and audit schema contracts.

## Verification performed

Executed successfully with Java 21:

```text
mvn -q -Dtest=HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentServiceTest,
TradeExecutionValidationServiceTest,
OpportunityConsolidationServiceTest,
BuyHtfAssessmentAuditStoreTest,
Fix154MigrationContractTest test
```

## Reviewer focus / remaining external proof

All FIX-154 implementation code described above is present in this local
workspace. The remaining release proof is execution, not an identified code
gap:

1. Run Flyway V107 against a dedicated disposable MySQL schema and verify
   `trade_signal`, `trade_signal_test`, and `buy_htf_assessment_audit`.
2. Run the full application/replay regression suite against that schema before
   Production deployment.
3. Confirm `flyway_schema_history` on the target database has not already
   applied V107; once applied, any correction must use a new forward migration.

No production database, service, or deployment configuration was changed by
this implementation work.

# Trade Investigation Agent

## Role

Investigate one Crypto AI Trader trade from durable evidence. Explain what
happened, why the system entered and exited, whether it followed the intended
rules, and whether the entry/exit was supported by the information available
at that time.

Investigations are read-only. Do not change code, configuration, services,
database records, review states, Replay work, or deployment state.

## Required input

Obtain or resolve:

- Environment and application: local, test, or production.
- Database host, port, and schema.
- One trade reference: `paper_position.id`, `wallet_trade.id`, or a symbol plus
  a bounded approximate entry-time window.
- Optional question or suspected issue.

Before analysis, confirm the symbol, trade/position ID, running version or FIX
level, and ingestion mode. Read the actual schema before choosing tables or
columns. Do not assume a table is in `crypto_ai` when it may belong to
`crypto_ai_v2`.

## Evidence rules

- Use configured database and SSH/log connections without displaying secrets.
- Use bounded, read-only queries and targeted log searches.
- Treat stored timestamps as UTC; display both UTC and KSA (`UTC+03:00`).
- Attach record IDs and timestamped log evidence to every factual conclusion.
- Distinguish **confirmed fact**, **supported inference**, and **missing or
  conflicting evidence**.
- Missing logs or records do not prove that no execution/effect occurred.
- A saved signal does not prove execution. A callback return does not prove a
  transaction committed. Under FIX-153, `market_price_event.APPLIED` proves a
  committed successful protection callback, but can be a no-op when there was
  no open position; find the position/trade mutation separately.
- Current configuration does not prove which settings were active at entry. If
  no configuration history exists, report that limitation.

## Investigation workflow

### 1. Resolve identity and environment

Link the trade reference to, where available:

- `paper_position`
- `wallet_trade`
- entry and exit `trade_signal`
- `signal_processing_work`
- wallet/position execution records

Record entry and exit times in UTC and KSA. Confirm whether the trader ran in
local-candle OFF mode or a legacy shared-market mode.

### 2. Collect a small evidence bundle

Start with exact IDs and a narrow time window. Expand only when the evidence
requires it. Collect:

| Evidence | Establishes |
| --- | --- |
| `paper_position` | lifecycle, entry/exit, stop, target, status |
| `wallet_trade` | executed quantity, prices, fees, linked signal |
| `trade_signal` | decision, strategy, scores, entry eligibility, reasons |
| `signal_processing_work` | claim, attempt, completion, expiry, uncertainty |
| `candle` / `technical_indicator` | market data and analysis inputs available then |
| `market_price_event` | intra-candle protection observations and outcomes |
| configuration/version evidence | applicable rules, when historically available |
| application and timing logs | analysis, execution, protection, exceptions, waits |

Avoid fetching large signal explanation fields until they are needed.

### 3. Reconstruct the timeline

Build a timeline, explicitly showing missing stages:

`candle closed D"+' signal saved D"+' work claimed D"+' locks acquired D"+' entry committed D"+' protection observations D"+' exit decision D"+' exit committed`

Measure separately:

- candle close to signal generation;
- signal generation to processing/entry;
- observation time to protection completion;
- exit-trigger evidence to recorded exit.

Correlate delays only with logs/events for the same symbol, position, signal,
or price-event identity. A nearby slow SQL query is not proof of causation.

### 4. Assess entry correctness and quality

Use only evidence that was available at entry. Check:

- signal mode, closed-candle identity, freshness, and data quality;
- final decision and entry-allowed result;
- BTC context, higher-timeframe, ATR, liquidity, risk/reward, sizing, and
  strategy gates that applied at that time;
- correct signal/position linkage and absence of duplicate/stale-retry entry;
- quantity, wallet balance, risk limits, fees, and fill/reference-price gap.

Return two separate conclusions:

- **Execution correctness:** followed or violated the implemented rules.
- **Entry quality:** supported, weak, or unverifiable from contemporaneous
  evidence.

Subsequent price movement may evaluate the outcome; it must never be presented
as information the entry decision had.

### 5. Reconstruct protection and stop-loss behavior

For FIX-153, inspect accepted `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` price events and retain
`PENDING`, `NON_CANONICAL`, and `PROTECTION_NOT_APPLIED` records as evidence
gaps or exclusions. Never substitute candle OHLC for absent intra-candle price
evidence.

For each relevant accepted observation:

1. Determine the then-active stop, target, trailing stop, and profit-lock state.
2. Find the first accepted observation that met an exit threshold.
3. Confirm that protection evaluated it and identify the resulting position or
   wallet execution record.
4. Explain delays, rejections, stale/out-of-order classification, exceptions,
   lock waits, or pool pressure.

Classify the exit path accurately: fixed stop-loss, trailing stop, profit-lock,
take-profit, signal-driven exit, manual exit, or unknown.

### 6. Assess exit correctness and quality

Compare the recorded exit reason, quantity, and price with trigger evidence.
Check threshold reach, accepted-observation identity, protection timing, and
the committed execution. Calculate maximum favourable/adverse movement during
the holding period separately from the contemporaneous exit assessment.

### 7. Reconcile financial results

Recalculate using execution records:

- bought/sold quantities and weighted entry/exit prices;
- gross P&L, fees, and net P&L;
- partial exits, remaining quantity, and realized versus unrealized values.

Flag discrepancies among wallet records, position records, and UI output.

### 8. Diagnose cause and use Replay carefully

Classify findings as one or more of:

- strategy decision;
- data freshness or coverage;
- analysis delay;
- execution failure;
- protection failure;
- database, lock, or connection-pool contention;
- accounting/UI discrepancy;
- insufficient evidence.

Run Replay only as a controlled comparison with equivalent candles, accepted
prices, strategy version, and settings. State every unavailable input or
material difference. Never claim parity by substituting candle OHLC for missing
price evidence or treating `PENDING` as accepted.

## Required final report

1. **Trade identity and environment** D"?" IDs, symbol, version/mode, scope.
2. **UTC/KSA timeline** D"?" evidence-backed sequence and latency breakdown.
3. **Entry** D"?" execution correctness and entry quality.
4. **Exit** D"?" execution correctness and exit quality.
5. **Stop-loss/profit-lock** D"?" worked, failed, or unverifiable, with evidence.
6. **P&L reconciliation** D"?" quantities, prices, fees, and discrepancies.
7. **Root cause and confidence** D"?" confirmed defect, valid loss, configuration
   issue, delay, or insufficient evidence.
8. **Evidence references** D"?" record IDs, query/log timestamps, code/config
   references.
9. **Missing evidence** D"?" exactly what prevents a stronger conclusion.
10. **Recommended correction and regression checks** D"?" only when justified.

A profitable trade can violate the rules, and a losing trade can be a correct
execution. State those independently.

# FIX-154 higher-timeframe BUY rule D"?" 2026-10-10

- `data_quality_valid=true` is required before a bearish 1h signal can be
  treated as trusted. `NULL` is unknown and fails closed for bearish evidence.
- A trusted `STRONG_SELL` at `confidence_score >= 70` blocks the BUY.
- A trusted `SELL`, or trusted `STRONG_SELL` below 70, is a SOFT conflict:
  profiles receive effective 1h `NEUTRAL`, and any allowed initial BUY is
  capped at 25%.
- A bearish 5m still returns `5M_BEARISH_VETO` before any 1h result.
- Existing progressive/add rules retain their raw 1h requirement; a SOFT 1h
  never authorizes an add.

# FIX-153 candle continuity rule D"?" 2026-10-10

- Missing price evidence never becomes invented OHLCV data and never bypasses
  the data-quality gate.
- Automatic reconciliation may restore only one exact, final Binance candle.
  Its recovered/backfilled row has no close token and cannot directly cause
  analysis, a signal, or a trade.
- A later normal live close is the only route that resumes ordinary signal
  generation after a repaired quality window.

# Signal-staleness root cause D"?" 2026-10-09

- System Health CRITICAL signal-staleness alerts were confirmed as accurate for 1m and 5m analysis, not a dashboard timezone issue. At the check, candles continued through `2026-10-09T06:43:00Z` (1m) and `06:35:00Z` (5m), while the newest live signals were `04:34:03Z` (1m) and `04:30:07Z` (5m).
- The most recent 210-candle data-quality window has a synchronized gap for every enabled symbol: missing 1m open `2026-10-09T04:34:00Z` (KSA 07:34) and missing 5m open `2026-10-09T04:30:00Z` (KSA 07:30).
- Cause confirmed from the WebSocket lifecycle log: generation 1 was revoked at `2026-10-09T04:35:00.133951100Z`; generation 2 was admitted at `04:35:01.369103400Z` (1,236 ms interruption). The affected 1m and 5m candles had both closed at the reconnect boundary. Binance did not replay their final closed-kline events, leaving the already-created local rows open rather than finalized.
- `CandleDataQualityService` is configured with `minimum-candles: 210` and `maximum-missing-candles: 0`. Therefore one missing candle makes the entire relevant analysis window invalid. Runtime logs confirm `DATA_QUALITY_BLOCKED` with `Missing 1 candle(s) in the analysis window` for current 1m and 5m worker attempts.
- 1h has no comparable gap and produced a live signal at `2026-10-09T06:00:03Z` (KSA 09:00).
- No repair or configuration change was made. Correct remediation must restore authoritative missing candles rather than relaxing the zero-missing data-quality safety rule.

# ETHUSDT paper-trade 59 investigation D"?" 2026-10-09

- Scope: local `crypto_ai` paper trade `wallet_trade.id=59` for `ETHUSDT`; all database reads were bounded and read-only. Stored UTC timestamps are shown below with their KSA (`UTC+03:00`) interpretation.
- Entry: signal `trade_signal.id=69226` was generated at `2026-10-09T07:33:00.233691Z` (10:33:00.234 KSA) from the 1m candle opened at 10:32 KSA. It was a `BUY`, score 79, confidence 70, entry allowed, at 2502.77. The trade executed at `07:33:01.654548Z` (10:33:01.655 KSA), 1.42 seconds after the signal; this is not an execution-latency issue.
- Exit: `wallet_trade.id=60` closed the position at 2504.30 at `07:34:34.196100Z` (10:34:34.196 KSA) with `PROFIT_LOCK_EXIT`. The managed position recorded a high of 2505.80 and an activated profit-lock price of 2504.429502243252. The realized paper gain was about 0.02445 USDT before fees (0.015982291620 ETH x 1.53 USDT).
- Market path: ETH rose from 2485.88 at 07:54 KSA to 2502.77 at entry (+16.89, +0.68%). It reached 2505.56 at 10:25 KSA before the entry; entry was 2.79 (0.11%) below that high. The post-entry high was 2505.93 at 10:34 KSA, only 3.16 (0.13%) above entry, then the candle retraced. This supports the user's observation that entry occurred late in the observed move.
- Primary cause: the local candle-quality interruption stopped 1m ETH signals after 07:34 KSA. There were no ETH 1m signals from 07:34 to 10:26 KSA, the period containing most of the rise. The interrupted 1m and 5m rows were repaired at about 10:28 KSA, so analysis resumed only near the later consolidation/breakout attempt. This is a confirmed data-availability gap, not proof that the strategy would have bought near 07:54 KSA.
- Immediate signal sequence after recovery: at 10:30 KSA the 5m signal was a BUY but blocked by `RANGE_ENTRY_LOCATION`; at 10:31 KSA the 1m raw BUY was vetoed by a bearish BTC context; at 10:33 KSA BTC context became neutral and the 1m trend-following BUY was allowed. The entry signal itself explicitly recorded mixed higher-timeframe context (5m BUY, 1h NEUTRAL) and a weakening but ultra-close ask wall before its target.
- Assessment: the trade followed the implemented rules once local analysis resumed, and the trade was entered/exited promptly. It was nevertheless a late entry relative to the full 07:54D"?"10:33 KSA advance because the system lacked signal coverage during the main move. The data gap is an operational defect already covered by the local-candle recovery deployment gate. Whether the unobserved earlier period would have produced an executable BUY is uncertain without a controlled Replay run; none was started because that would create replay state.
# SHIBUSDT signal #89469 / position #35 D"?" 2026-10-10

- Scope: read-only local `crypto_ai` investigation of wallet trades `106`/`107`,
  paper position `35`, managed position `64`, and signal `89469`.
- Timestamp correction (2026-10-10): the preceding interpretation was wrong
  because the read-only investigation connection used MySQL's `SYSTEM` timezone
  (KSA), while the application connection explicitly sets its session to UTC.
  For position `35`, MySQL's KSA session renders `opened_at` as
  `2026-10-09 23:45:06.682175`; converting that instant to UTC yields
  `2026-10-09 20:45:06.682175`. The Inspector's main trade card parses backend
  timestamps as UTC and renders them in the browser-local timezone. Therefore,
  with a KSA browser, its displayed 23:45 entry time is correct. Do not add
  three hours to the value shown by this KSA-session SQL connection.
- Signal confirmation: `trade_signal.id=89469` was generated at
  `2026-10-09 23:45:03.498678` KSA (`20:45:03.498678` UTC), from the 1m
  candle opened at 23:44 KSA. It is `WATCH`, score 60, confidence 74,
  `WEAK_UPTREND`, `TREND_FOLLOWING`, and `final_entry_allowed=1`.
- Entry: `0.000005410`; stop `0.000005382950`; TP `0.000005433730`; the
  25%-size setup-wakeup route entered from a 5m BUY with 1h NEUTRAL context.
- Exit: highest recorded price before close was `0.000005430`, or 84.2815% of
  the original TP distance. The dynamic lock activated at `0.000005421865` and
  live price `0.000005420` breached it. At the breach the current 1m and 5m
  were WATCH and 1h was NEUTRAL. The shared exit policy therefore returned
  `PROFIT_LOCK_EXIT`; it requires bullish 1h plus supportive 5m, or WATCH 1h
  plus supportive 5m, to hold a breached lock.
- TP extension: not evaluated. Extension begins only once price is at or above
  the original TP. The recorded high remained below TP at the time of exit.
- Subsequent action: later local candles reached `0.000005500` at about 00:40
  KSA, above the original TP. This is hindsight, not evidence available at the
  exit. Local SHIB 1m/5m coverage ends at about 00:56 KSA, so any claimed
  continuation through 12:15 KSA is not verifiable from local candle evidence.
- Assessment: behavior followed the existing protection rule, without a
  confirmed execution or latency defect. A future exit-policy change could
  explicitly allow a near-TP WATCH/WATCH/NEUTRAL continuation hold under
  measurable momentum/structure conditions and a tighter safety floor; it
  requires a separate design and Replay validation.

# SHIBUSDT #89469 near-TP continuation investigation D"?" 2026-10-10

- Status: investigation approved; implementation is explicitly not approved.
  This workstream must not alter or delay FIX-153 release work.
- Hypothesis to test: a breached profit lock close to target may sometimes
  continue when current market structure and momentum remain supportive.
- Important counter-hypothesis: `1h=NEUTRAL` is not positive continuation
  evidence merely because 1m/5m are `WATCH`. Relaxing that guard on distance
  to TP alone could create false holds in weak or sideways markets.
- Required evidence before a policy change: use historical Replay/production
  evidence to compare the current exit rule against a hypothetical bounded
  hold using only information available at the breach. Segment results by
  1h/5m context, regime, trend structure, momentum, retracement depth and
  target-progress band. Evaluate TP hit rate, retained profit, adverse move,
  drawdown, and false-hold frequency. No post-exit price may inform entry or
  exit eligibility.
- Rejected approach: do not authorize continuation from `WATCH/WATCH/NEUTRAL`
  or near-TP distance alone.
- Code-review refinement: `PositionExitPolicy` creates a sharp tier boundary:
  with supportive 5m/current evidence, 1h `WATCH` can hold a breached lock but
  1h `NEUTRAL` falls through to `PROFIT_LOCK_EXIT`. Existing FIX-11T near-TP
  protection arms at 90% target progress and is evaluated after the ordinary
  lock-breach exit, so it could not affect #89469 at 84.28% progress.
- Fee-model refinement: `WalletAutoExecutionService` writes `fee_usdt=0` for
  paper BUY, SELL, and near-TP partial-harvest executions. Any policy Replay
  comparison must add an explicit fee/slippage model before claiming protected
  gross profit is a net gain.
- Approved experiment protocol from independent review: Production policy is
  unchanged. Compare (A) existing immediate lock exit, (B) a one-window,
  near-TP continuation using existing time-safe structure/momentum/context
  calculations, and (C) a volatility-adjusted tighter trailing exit. Use a
  pre-registered 85% TP-progress primary threshold; #89469 at 84.28% does not
  qualify and must not drive threshold selection. Require non-bearish 5m/1h,
  a fee-adjusted protected floor with modeled adverse fills, explicit numeric
  momentum/retracement definitions, no timer resets or repeated extensions,
  and strictly as-of-breach evidence. Evaluate net expectancy, drawdown,
  false-hold/adverse-move rate, and include later-decline counterexamples;
  reserve an out-of-sample period before any implementation decision.
- Final protocol controls: calculate TP progress only from immutable original
  entry and original TP; exclude and report zero/negative/invalid target
  distance. Every A/B/C candidate must use identical historical data,
  evaluation periods, and fee/slippage assumptions. Candidate B must model an
  intrawindow price path sufficiently to determine which of its emergency
  floor or target was reached first; candle high/low alone is not evidence of
  that order. Pre-register the minimum sample size, required net-expectancy
  improvement, and maximum allowed drawdown deterioration before results are
  viewed, then report the qualification count for the B cohort.
- Final cohort and comparison definition: Candidate B is eligible only in the
  85% to strictly-less-than-90% original-TP-progress band, with fresh,
  quality-valid 1h `NEUTRAL`, supportive fresh/quality-valid 5m, and required
  current-frame structure/momentum evidence. Missing, stale, invalid, or
  unknown 1h/5m evidence makes the case ineligible rather than non-bearish.
  This avoids changing or competing with existing FIX-11T behaviour at 90%.
  Pre-count the eligible cohort; insufficient sample means keep collecting.
  B-versus-A is the sole primary comparison; C is secondary/exploratory and
  must have its trailing formula fixed before Replay. Verify ATR, spread, and
  tick-size availability as of breach; if historical spread is unavailable,
  use a declared fixed assumption and sensitivity analysis.
- Final breach semantics: eligibility uses `breach_tp_progress`, calculated
  from the executable price at the exact profit-lock breach, not the historic
  high-water mark. Persist/report both `max_tp_progress` and
  `breach_tp_progress`. The continuation emergency floor is distinct from the
  breached original lock; if actual executable price (including modeled
  fee/spread/slippage) already violates that floor, exit immediately. Freeze
  numerical precision, 90%-boundary precedence (FIX-11T owns 90%+), hold
  length, tolerance, floor, costs, sample threshold, and success criteria
  before inspecting results. Report confidence intervals and chronological
  out-of-sample results; a small qualifying cohort is insufficient evidence.
- Team-2 final validity controls: create and commit a numerical pre-registration
  before any outcome is viewed, recording its immutable content hash here.
  Verify available intrabar path data; where tick/price-event evidence cannot
  order TP versus emergency-floor contact, mark the case ambiguous, apply one
  conservative adverse-move-first rule consistently, and report analyses both
  with and without those cases. Compute the floor per trade as
  `max(original_lock - tolerance, fee_adjusted_breakeven)` and report when
  breakeven binds. Compare A and B as paired outcomes on the same eligible
  breach events, reporting per-event B-minus-A and the count of later declines
  avoided by A.

# Replay-versus-Production parity audit D"?" 2026-10-10

- Scope: source-level comparison of the manual Regression/Replay pipeline with
  the current paper-trading Production path. No trading code, configuration,
  database data, or Replay result was changed.
- Confirmed shared authority: Replay regenerates technical indicators through
  `TechnicalIndicatorService`, signals through `AnalysisService`, BUY authority
  through `ExecutionIntelligenceService`, BUY/SELL validation through
  `TradeExecutionValidationService`, and normal exit, TP-continuation,
  profit-lock, and near-TP policies through the corresponding shared policy
  classes. When canonical `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` price events exist, the Replay
  consumes them in timestamp order before making the same close-candle signal
  visible, matching the documented live ordering.
- Confirmed boundary: Replay is a shadow simulation, not a transactionally
  identical copy of Production. It starts with fixed capital of 10,000 USDT,
  manages one symbol/position in isolation, uses replay-local daily budget and
  position state, and writes only `*_test` tables. It therefore cannot
  reproduce an actual wallet balance, simultaneous positions, global
  `maxOpenPositions`, daily loss limit, idempotency races, database locks, or
  real execution latency.
- Confirmed historical-evidence limitation: derivatives history is not
  persisted, so historical Replay always receives `UNAVAILABLE` derivatives
  context when that feature is enabled. Historical order-book evaluation uses
  stored snapshots only and becomes `UNAVAILABLE` when no snapshot exists,
  whereas live Production samples its in-memory/live Binance source. Such runs
  are useful simulations but are not full signal-parity proof unless the
  required historical evidence exists and is reported.
- Confirmed price-evidence limitation: a Replay run labels itself
  `EXACT_PRICE_REPLAY` whenever at least one accepted price event exists. It
  does not prove complete tick coverage for every time an open position needed
  protection. Windows with no accepted events fall back to signal prices and
  are labelled `SIGNAL_PRICE_FALLBACK`; neither fallback nor partial coverage
  should be described as tick-exact reproduction.
- Test evidence: Java 21 focused tests passed for profit-lock/TP-extension
  parity, position exit/continuation, near-TP policy, and shared sizing.
  Existing Replay-specific coverage is narrow: it does not execute a complete
  Production-versus-Replay scenario across entry, add, wallet controls,
  price-event ordering, partial harvest, and exit.
- Conclusion: Replay is suitable for controlled, manual strategy regression
  and the approved FIX-155 research work only when each run carries explicit
  evidence-quality labels. It is not yet valid to claim it is fully identical
  to Production in all phases. A future parity program must add deterministic
  scenario tests and a run-level eligibility contract before using Replay as a
  release-equivalence certificate.

- Implemented: OFF-mode trader-owned candle ingestion, monotonic close tokens,
  bounded detector/lanes, canonical price evidence, direct-attempt freshness,
  REST exchange-time close validation, and controlled ADMIN drain behavior.
- Operational impact: a successful drain exits the JVM; a timeout resumes
  admission. Price protection pauses during a drain window.

  production migration or data change was run by Codex.
- Release status: NOT APPROVED FOR PRODUCTION. Java 21, disposable-MySQL,

# 2026-10-08 D"?" FIX-153 exchange/database clock correction

- Problem: JVM/database clock skew could make a Binance-derived candle appear
  too early or too old during freshness checks.
- Solution: `ExchangeClockSynchronizer` measures Binance server time against
  database UTC and JVM time at startup and periodically; database and JVM
  callers apply their own bounded offset without HTTP inside locks.
- The clock-skew warning uses the larger absolute database or JVM offset; the
  dedicated Binance time client has a five-second connect/read bound.
- General Binance REST requests now use the configured request timeout, and
  scheduled clock sampling cannot monopolise the four-thread scheduler pool.
- Bootstrap REST reads occur before its local candle-write transaction, so
  the bounded Binance timeout does not hold a database transaction open.

# 2026-10-09 D"?" FIX-153 audit: analysis outcomes and protection clock

- Audit result: normal live and historical analysis persist a `TradeSignal`,
  including neutral/no-entry decisions; there is no normal analysed-without-
  durable-outcome path to reconcile.
- Corrected the post-lock direct-protection timestamp validation to use the
  JVM-to-exchange clock correction, matching initial receipt validation.
- A universal shutdown barrier remains a separate, unverified design task:
  it must cover financial mutations without treating unrelated administration
  or configuration writes as trading authority.
- Database impact: none. Production and Replay impact: execution freshness now
  follows an automatically measured exchange-aligned reference clock.
- Validation still required: unavailable-time-source, stale observation,
  positive/negative skew, and first-attempt freshness regression tests.

  repair authoritative interrupted-open candles and exact absent identities in
  the active quality window. Inserted identities use `BACKFILL` provenance and
  never receive a close token or dispatch live analysis.
- No manual approval, database repair, or persisted review fence may be needed
  for pending candle/analysis reconciliation. Unverified data remains explicit
  non-executable evidence; this direction does not override legitimate
  strategy or risk-entry vetoes.
- Status: design only; no database migration, code, Production, or Replay
  behavior has changed.

# 2026-10-09 D"?" Appendix K reconciliation contract alignment

- Project guidance now records the approved pending design constraints: pure
  insert-only authoritative gap backfill; quality-window-bounded candidates;
  durable terminal outcomes; structurally isolated historical processing; a
  shared Binance REST rate-limit gate; and durable financial-effect
  reconciliation only after atomicity is proven.
- The planned universal mutation barrier is explicitly in-memory and active
  only during drain; it must not queue, delay, or persistently fence fresh
  trading outside drain. Protection for open positions during replacement is
  still an unresolved release gate.
- Status: documentation/design alignment only. No runtime behavior, schema,
  Production data, or Replay behavior was changed.

# 2026-10-09 D"?" FIX-153 automatic reconciliation implementation (initial slice)

- Problem: reconnect-boundary or startup candle gaps could leave local history
  invalid until a person performed a database repair.
- Solution: added V107 durable reconciliation/audit and terminal-outcome
  tables; guarded update-only authoritative recovery; pure insert-only gap
  backfill; a dedicated recovery executor; reconnect/scheduled requests;
  token-free recovery outcomes; post-ingestion late-live-close observation;
  and reconciliation participation in local drain.
- Files/classes changed: `CandleRepository`, `BinanceMarketDataClient`,
  `BinanceKlineService`, `BinanceWebSocketManager`, `LocalDrainService`,
  `LocalCandleHealth`, `SignalProcessingStore`, plus new local reconciliation,
  outcome, audit, executor-config, and V107 migration files.

  impact: feature is disabled by default and has not been deployed. Universal
  mutation-barrier coverage, shared REST admission/rate-limit control,
  quarantined-effect reconciliation, MySQL crash/race tests, and Replay parity
  remain open release work.

# 2026-10-09 D"?" FIX-153 reconciliation outcome correction

- Corrected recovery semantics so `SKIPPED_NOT_LIVE` is recorded only when a
  recovery/backfill actually changes candle evidence; a competing live or
  existing row is not relabelled.
- `REST_NOT_FINAL` is now retryable rather than a terminal mismatch. Audit keys
  use fixed epoch-millisecond identity precision, and reconciliation is safely
  disabled by default even when configuration is absent.
- Added terminal outcomes for worker data-unavailable, historical-only,
  already-analysed, stale work, and detector expiry paths. Transient failures
  remain without a terminal outcome so retry remains possible.
- Added `LocalReconciliationAuditRepositoryTest`; targeted Java 21 tests and
  compile passed. MySQL, race/crash, rate-limit, barrier, effect-reconciler,
  and Replay validation remain open.

# 2026-10-09 D"?" FIX-153 reconciliation safety correction

- Recovery now requires positive loss evidence before it may finalise an open
  row: either the successor candle is already present or a reconnect was
  admitted after the candidate close. This prevents a short healthy-stream
  delay from being converted into a token-free, unanalysed close.
- REST finality/identity disagreement is retryable while the local row remains
  open. The authoritative Binance close timestamp, not the local expected
  value, is persisted after a successful exact match. A bounded first-candle
  probe identifies pre-listing identities as explicit unavailable outcomes.
- Recovery uses the short timeout client and checks drain between requests;
  late-live-close evidence now preserves its first timestamp.
- Status: Java 21 compilation passed. Dedicated reconciliation race, drain,
  MySQL, and Replay tests remain required before enabling the feature.

# 2026-10-09 D"?" FIX-153 deterministic reconciliation test coverage

  late live close without successor/reconnect evidence is left untouched;
  successor evidence permits update-only repair and records a non-live outcome;
  a gap-insert race ends as `ALREADY_PRESENT` without inventing an outcome;
  a confirmed exchange gap ends as `SKIPPED_DATA_UNAVAILABLE`; and active drain
  prevents REST admission.
- Corrected the audit test to use a true terminal state (`EXPIRED`) rather than
  `REST_MISMATCH`, which is deliberately retryable.

# FIX-154 D"?" Implementation Handoff for Code Review

Date: 2026-10-10  
Status: implemented in the local working tree; not deployed.

## Scope

FIX-154 changes only higher-timeframe BUY authority. It does not enable or
change FIX-153 reconciliation/runtime behavior.

The objective is to distinguish trusted, strong bearish 1h evidence from
unknown or weaker evidence without changing a stored historical signal.

## Implemented policy

1. The current 1m BUY still requires fresh 5m and 1h context.
2. A bearish 5m returns the existing `5M_BEARISH_VETO` first.
3. A bearish 1h with `data_quality_valid` `NULL` or `false` returns
   `1H_BEARISH_VETO`.
4. A trusted 1h `STRONG_SELL` with `confidence_score >= 70` returns
   `HTF_TRUSTED_BEARISH_VETO`.
5. A trusted 1h `SELL`, or `STRONG_SELL` with confidence below 70, is a
   SOFT conflict. It passes effective 1h `NEUTRAL` to profile logic and caps
   every allowed initial entry at 25%.
6. A non-bearish 1h with unknown quality keeps its raw decision; FIX-154 does
   not tighten bullish/non-bearish authority merely because historical quality
   is absent.
7. Existing progressive/add logic retains its raw 1h requirement. A SOFT
   conflict cannot authorize an add. Optional `SOFT_HTF_ADD_BLOCKED` evidence
   is observational only.

## Database changes

`V107__fix_154_trusted_higher_timeframe_buy.sql` adds:

- `trade_signal.data_quality_valid BOOLEAN NULL`.
- Replay parity columns on `trade_signal_test`:
  `btc_context_candle_close_time`, `btc_context_age_seconds`,
  `btc_context_freshness_threshold_seconds`, `signal_mode`, and
  `data_quality_valid`.
- Append-only `buy_htf_assessment_audit`, including effective/raw decisions,
  candle-close-based age, quality evidence, the hard-confirmation marker,
  cap, legacy veto and final result.

Historical `data_quality_valid` stays `NULL`; no value is backfilled.

## Included production code

| File | Change |
|---|---|
| `domain/TradeSignal.java` | Maps nullable `data_quality_valid`. |
| `service/AnalysisService.java` | Persists `MarketContextSnapshot.dataValid()` at signal generation. |
| `service/HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentService.java` | Pure FIX-154 decision matrix; returns detached effective decisions and never mutates entities. |
| `service/TradeExecutionValidationService.java` | Applies assessment to direct BUY and contextual/accumulated authority; enforces SOFT cap; records non-blocking audit evidence. |
| `service/OpportunityConsolidationService.java` | Applies the same matrix and cap to consolidation. |
| `execution/service/ExecutionIntelligenceService.java` | Keeps existing progressive raw-1h guard; invokes optional audit-only SOFT evidence after its rejection. Uses `SETUP_WAKEUP` audit path for the 5m wake-up caller. |
| `service/BuyHtfAssessmentAuditStore.java` | Snapshots and asynchronously persists best-effort Production audit rows. Replay writes no Production audit row. |
| `config/Fix154AuditAsyncConfig.java` | Dedicated one-thread, bounded executor; audit saturation drops evidence rather than borrowing a trading connection. |
| `service/Fix154Metrics.java` | Non-gating counters for untrusted 1h blocks, audit failures, SOFT caps and SOFT add blocks. |
| `service/Fix154DiagnosticsController.java` | Read-only `GET /api/fix154/health` metrics endpoint. |
| `service/Fix154RuntimeSchemaChecks.java` | Checks V107-required production and Replay schema before runtime admission. |

## Replay behavior

The regular reflection-based Replay writer persists every JPA `TradeSignal`
column. V107 therefore updates `trade_signal_test` alongside production.
FIX-154 audit rows are deliberately skipped while `ExecutionReplayScope` is
active because replay-generated entities do not have Production IDs and parity
is recorded by ReplayD"?Ts own evidence tables.

  - hard, soft, untrusted, 5m-veto precedence, 69/70 boundary, BNB #150a and
    #150b policy cases, and unknown non-bearish authority.
- `TradeExecutionValidationServiceTest`
  - direct/context SOFT caps, conservative behavior, neutral-5m exception is
    not unlocked, audit failure isolation, Production/Replay parity, and
    future-context exclusion.
- `OpportunityConsolidationServiceTest`
  - SOFT cap through consolidation.
- `BuyHtfAssessmentAuditStoreTest`
  - Replay does not write a Production audit row.
- `Fix154MigrationContractTest`
  - V107 contains production, Replay and audit schema contracts.
