# Crypto AI Trader ’'?" Architecture

## FIX-156 Replay boundary correction ’'?" 2026-10-16

Replay has a dedicated `com.crypto.regression.replay` boundary foundation. It
holds the frozen policy-profile contract, Replay-only sizing/minimum-order/SELL
policy primitives, and an operation-aware gateway for the narrowly permitted
`fix122_evaluation` diagnostic operations. This foundation remains incomplete:
the existing shadow Replay workflow has not yet been fully rewired away from
wallet settings, live Binance constraints, shared BUY validation, or legacy
test-table output. Production runtime and trading behavior remain unchanged.

V110 was confirmed already applied and was restored unchanged. Any new
Replay-owned schema is forward-only V111; it creates profile-detail, run
certificate, and price-coverage records without changing an existing table.

The active baseline no longer reads the legacy shared `fix122_replay_revision`
experiment switch, and its manual chart does not query `wallet_trade`. These
are boundary corrections only; the Replay-local BUY/lifecycle mirror and all
parity evidence remain pending.

`ReplaySizingPolicy`, `ReplayMinimumExecutionPolicy`, and the existing
`ReplaySellValidationPolicy` are Spring-managed Replay-only components because
they are injected by `ShadowProductionReplayService`; they have no wallet or
live-Binance dependency.

Status labels used in this document:

- **Current** ’'?" present in the repository today.
- **Pending** ’'?" approved design direction, not implemented or deployed.
- **Legacy/shared** ’'?" existing shared-market path retained as historical evidence
  while the trader moves to local-candle ownership.

## Current release status

**FIX-153 is implemented in source but is NOT APPROVED FOR PRODUCTION.**
It moves the trader toward collector-free, trader-owned local candle ingestion
in `shared-market.mode=OFF`. Java 21, disposable-MySQL, crash/race, Windows
two-process, soak, OFF-mode and Replay-parity proof gates remain open.

FIX-154 is a separate pending higher-timeframe design. It is not implemented
and is not part of FIX-153 deployment scope.

**FIX-156 is in progress and Replay-only.** A foundation exists for immutable
Replay policy profiles, Replay-owned evidence/output tables, and a fail-closed
Replay SQL allow-list. The existing Replay service has not yet been rewired
away from its live wallet/Binance/shared-service dependencies, so FIX-156 is
not complete or deployable.

## System purpose

The application is a Spring Boot cryptocurrency trader that analyses Binance
market data, persists candles and technical indicators, creates trade signals,
and performs paper-wallet execution and position protection. Production and
Replay are intended to use equivalent persisted market evidence.

## Runtime and data ownership

| Area | Current ownership | Target direction |
|---|---|---|
| Trader database | `crypto_ai` | Remains the trader's only runtime database. |
| Collector database | `crypto_ai_v2` | Collector remains separate and is not read/written by the trader at runtime. |
| Candle ingestion | In OFF mode, trader-owned Binance ingestion writes local `crypto_ai.candle`. | Must be proven in a collector-free deployment rehearsal. |
| Candle processing | Bounded lanes, close-transition tokens and a local detector are implemented. | MySQL/race/crash proof remains required. |
| Trading | Paper wallet and managed positions. | Live-exchange orders are outside the local-candle automatic-resolution design. |

## Current primary flow

```text
Binance WebSocket kline
  -> BinanceKlineService
  -> KlineTransactionCoordinator
      -> input transaction: candle + canonical 1m market price event
      -> protection transaction: LivePositionProtectionService
      -> observer transaction
      -> close-dispatch transaction
  -> CandleClosedEvent (AFTER_COMMIT)
  -> CandleAnalysisDispatcher
  -> CandleClosedAnalysisWorker
  -> TechnicalIndicatorService
  -> AnalysisService / TradeSignal
  -> SignalProcessingCoordinator
  -> PaperTradingService / wallet and managed-position state
```

`KlineTransactionCoordinator` preserves input -> protection -> observer ->
close-dispatch ordering. Closed-candle analysis is asynchronous and must not
run on the Binance ingestion thread.

## Current core persistence

| Table/domain | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `candle` | Local OHLCV candle history keyed by symbol, interval, and open time. |
| `technical_indicator` | Persisted indicator snapshots derived from closed candles. |
| `trade_signal` | Persisted analysis decisions and their context. |
| `signal_processing_work` | Durable processing registration/status for a persisted signal. |
| `market_price_event` | Canonical 1m price evidence used by protection and Replay. |
| Paper wallet / position tables | Paper executions, open-position state, TP/SL/profit protection. |

## Legacy/shared-market path

**Current but pending retirement from the trader runtime.**

The shared path includes `SharedMarketSource`, `SharedMarketConsumer`,
`SharedAnalysisDispatcher`, cutover/readiness logic, and local historical
tables such as `shared_market_event_delivery`, `shared_market_consumer_state`,
and `shared_market_health`. These records remain evidence and must not be
dropped, reset, or used to manufacture an approval.

The collector event/cursor source is `crypto_ai_v2.market_data_stream_event`
and `market_data_stream_cursor`. In the target local-candle runtime, the
trader must not connect to, read, or write these collector tables.

`V103__fix_149_fresh_admission_indexes` is an existing Java Flyway migration
that requires the collector event table for a fresh installation. A trader-only
bootstrap policy is therefore **pending** and remains a release blocker. Do
not edit applied Flyway migrations or Flyway history.

## Implemented, unverified: local-candle architecture (FIX-153)

The implemented intended mode is `shared-market.mode=OFF` with trader-owned Binance kline
ingestion and local candle writes. The shared executors and direct-ingestion
path must never run together.

Implemented safeguards:

- Candle writes are monotonic: a late open update cannot reopen or overwrite a
  closed candle.
- A closed-candle transition is admitted exactly once using an atomic upsert
  result plus in-process admission coordination.
- A bounded local detector recovers a closed candle that was committed before
  the in-memory event/dispatcher could start analysis.
- Fresh candles may execute; stale candles are analysed historically only, or
  their already-owned work is expired without business execution.
- Analysis lanes are bounded. Under overload, 1m may retain only the latest
  pending candle; higher intervals use an explicit bounded FIFO policy.
- Historic shared `REVIEW_REQUIRED` evidence must not block OFF-mode work.
- OFF-mode health/admin endpoints must use local-candle health and must not
  query retired shared-market tables.
- Current REST backfill can finalise an interrupted open row but cannot mint a
  runtime close token or dispatch analysis. Runtime close tokens plus
  provenance keep backfill out of detector admission. The approved, pending
  automatic-reconciliation extension will also insert an exact wholly absent
  candle only inside the active quality window, with `BACKFILL` provenance and
  the same no-token/no-dispatch boundary.
- Observed local behavior (2026-10-09): a WebSocket reconnect at a candle-close
  boundary can leave the already-persisted 1m/5m rows open because Binance does
  not replay the final closed-kline event. The current detector recovers missing
  analysis admission, not missing close facts. An authoritative, bounded REST
  repair may therefore finalise the exact `closed=0` row or, under the approved
  gap-backfill scope, insert an exact absent identity. It must preserve existing
  provenance, use `BACKFILL` for an insertion, create no close-transition token,
  and never dispatch historical work. Automatic reconciliation of this reconnect
  case remains a FIX-153 release gate; the one-time local repair is evidence,
  not proof that the gap is closed.
- Approved pending reconciliation design: gap backfill is insert-only (no
  duplicate-key update); it can never alter a live row that appears during a
  REST request. Candidate selection is bounded to the active quality window;
  terminal audit records are not retried. Every candle/work route must produce
  a durable terminal outcome or remain explicitly retryable after a transient
  failure. Historical-only processing has no execution authority.
- The pending universal mutation barrier is an in-memory drain participant,
  not a database or persistent trading fence. Outside drain it must not delay,
  queue, or block fresh trading, signals, or protection. Open-position
  protection during application replacement remains an unresolved release gate.
- Local source now contains the first reconciliation implementation slice:
  V107 durable audit/outcome tables, update-only authoritative open-row repair,
  pure insert-only gap backfill, a dedicated recovery executor, a reconnect
  request hook, and drain participation. It is disabled by default and remains
  unvalidated on MySQL; it has no deployment approval.
- Direct prices are normalised to `DECIMAL(30,12)` before persistence,
  protection and completion. Equal timestamps have one canonical receipt.
- Every processing attempt is freshness-checked after normal locks using the
  database-to-exchange correction. JVM-origin timestamp decisions use the
  separately measured JVM-to-exchange correction. The measurement performs no
  HTTP under execution locks and uses a bounded five-second time-only client.
  General Binance REST calls use the configured request timeout, and scheduled
  clock sampling has a four-thread Spring scheduler pool.
  Bootstrap obtains time/klines before opening its local candle-write
  transaction, so a stalled REST request does not retain database resources.
  REST bootstrap gets Binance server time before klines.
- An ADMIN-only drain stops analysis admission, exits the JVM on successful
  quiesce, and resumes admission on timeout. Price protection pauses during
  the controlled drain window.

## Implemented, unverified: protection price-event state machine

For direct 1m price observations, the target evidence lifecycle is:

```text
PENDING
  -> APPLIED                 (protection effects and state commit together)
  -> NON_CANONICAL           (invalid, stale, out-of-order, or duplicate)
  -> PROTECTION_NOT_APPLIED  (confirmed rollback/crash resolution)
```

Replay and price authority consume only accepted `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` rows.
Replay must reject a parity claim when the requested range contains unresolved
direct `PENDING` evidence; this must not block live trading.

The automatic resolver is valid only for the paper-trading phase, where every
protection effect commits in the same transaction as its `APPLIED` update.
Before deployment, the code must prove that there are no asynchronous,
`REQUIRES_NEW`, in-memory-only, or external effects in this boundary. A future
live-exchange order needs a separate idempotent/exchange-reconciliation design.

The target runtime also requires exactly one active trader process. This is an
operational prerequisite, not a database lease or fence.

## Pending: FIX-154 higher-timeframe BUY validation

FIX-154 is separate from the local-candle change and is not implemented.

Its purpose is to let a historical bearish 1h signal affect a current 1m BUY
only when the historical evidence is fresh, quality-valid, and sufficiently
strong. `btcContextEntryAllowed=false` on a historical signal is investigatory
evidence, not a higher-timeframe quality gate; the current 1m BTC protection
remains unchanged.

The design includes reliability bands (`NOT_TRUSTED`, `SOFT_CONFLICT`,
`HARD_BEARISH_CANDIDATE`), required fresh/quality-valid bearish 5m support for
a hard veto, persisted `trade_signal.data_quality_valid`, recovery release
rules, immutable outcome audit evidence, and Replay/no-look-ahead parity.

The implementation design is maintained in `codex-docs/FIX-154_TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md`.
It supersedes no existing deployed behavior and is deliberately separate from
the unrelated legacy readiness document under `src/main/resources/md/FIX-146.md`.

## Deployment posture

Neither FIX-153 nor FIX-154 is deployment-ready. FIX-153 source hardening is
implemented but unverified; FIX-154 remains pending.

Required validation includes Java 21 build/tests, disposable MySQL bootstrap,
real-MySQL concurrency and lock-wait tests, crash/restart tests, stale-input
tests, Replay parity checks, OFF-mode health/admin checks, collector-free
runtime verification, deterministic dispatcher/detector/drain tests,
two-process Windows lock rehearsal, and sustained-load observation across
enabled symbols and intervals. This local workspace has Java 17 and cannot
compile the Java 21 project.

No production configuration, database, service, or deployment change is made
without explicit user approval.

## FIX-153 validation status (2026-10-09)

The reconciliation component now has deterministic Java 21 coverage for its
core admission and outcome contract. The tests execute the real audit and
outcome stores against H2 while mocking only Binance and the candle write
boundary. They prove that healthy delayed live closes are not taken over and
that recovery remains token-free/non-live when it is positively evidenced.

The real-MySQL suite contains matching update-only and insert-only assertions,
but it requires a deliberately disposable loopback MySQL instance and is not
run by the ordinary unit-test goal. This is a release gate, not a warning that
can be waived. The shared Binance 429/418 admission gate, universal mutation
barrier, structural historical-only isolation, financial-effect reconciler,
atomicity/crash proofs, replacement-time position protection, Replay parity,
and soak validation remain incomplete. Therefore `local-candle.reconciliation.enabled`
must stay `false`.

The single `local-candle` configuration mapping is a startup invariant. It now
contains both reconciliation and existing local-runtime properties; a second
mapping is invalid YAML and prevents the Spring application context from
starting.

### Shared Binance REST admission

`BinanceRestAdmissionGate` owns one process-wide REST cooldown. It is consulted
immediately before every call through `BinanceMarketDataClient`; a 429/418
response extends the cooldown using `Retry-After` when it is supplied, otherwise
the bounded fallback. This protects all local REST consumers from repeatedly
calling Binance during a rate-limit/ban response. It is deliberately not part
of the WebSocket or protection path and is not a persisted trading fence.

### Outcome-schema startup invariant

The active signal-processing runtime writes `candle_analysis_outcome` for
terminal analysis evidence. V108 therefore ships with the FIX-154 release even
though local-candle reconciliation remains disabled. `CandleAnalysisOutcomeSchemaVerifier`
runs after Flyway and fails application startup with a clear message if the
table is absent; this prevents later processing transactions from failing only
when they attempt their first outcome write.
# FIX-154 implementation update ’'?" 2026-10-10

`HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentService` is the single pure assessment boundary for
the 5m/1h BUY context. It returns effective decisions separately from the JPA
entities, so a SOFT 1h conflict can be assessed as effective `NEUTRAL` without
ever changing the persisted signal. `TradeExecutionValidationService` uses it
for direct BUY and accumulated-evidence authority; `OpportunityConsolidationService`
uses the same assessment for consolidation. A trusted SOFT bearish 1h limits an
initial entry to 25%; a trusted HARD or unknown bearish 1h rejects it, while
the legacy 5m bearish veto keeps priority.

`buy_htf_assessment_audit` is append-only operational evidence. Its writer is
best-effort and uses a bounded dedicated executor: auditing never borrows an
execution transaction's database connection, is skipped in Replay, and cannot
be part of a BUY decision. The progressive add route retains its
existing raw-1h guard; it writes `SOFT_HTF_ADD_BLOCKED` evidence only after
that guard has already rejected an add.

`GET /api/fix154/health` exposes non-gating counters for untrusted bearish
blocks, soft caps, soft add blocks, and best-effort audit failures.

## FIX-153 automatic local-candle reconciliation ’'?" enabled 2026-10-10

In `shared-market.mode=OFF`, `LocalCandleReconciliationService` runs every
five seconds on its dedicated single-worker executor. It finds eligible
missing or interrupted candle identities inside the data-quality window, reads
the authoritative Binance candle, and writes only verified final evidence.
An existing open row is finalized by an update-only path; a missing row uses a
plain insert-if-absent backfill path. Neither route creates a close token,
dispatches analysis, or executes a trade. The next genuine live close resumes
ordinary analysis after the quality window is complete.

# FIX-154 ’'?" Trusted higher-timeframe BUY validation

Status: implementation design approved. FIX-154 is scheduled before the
remaining FIX-153 runtime release work.

## Purpose and boundary

FIX-154 replaces the current binary fresh-1h bearish veto with a trusted
higher-timeframe assessment. It does not relax the existing 5m confirmation
requirement, risk/reward result, current-1m BTC protection, or profile rules
except where a raw 1h decision would otherwise act as an untrusted veto.

The legacy `src/main/resources/md/FIX-146.md` is an unrelated shared-market
readiness design and is not an implementation source for this fix.

## Current integration points to replace

The 1h/5m blocks in all three paths below must be replaced as a whole by one
shared assessment call. Do not add this decision to `FinalDecisionService`.

1. `TradeExecutionValidationService.validateBuy()` ’'?" direct 1m BUYs.
2. `TradeExecutionValidationService.validateBuyContext()` ’'?" accumulated/setup
   authority, whose reference may be a 5m signal.
3. `OpportunityConsolidationService.evaluate()` ’'?" consolidated BUYs.

Each replacement owns the current lookup, freshness, 5m veto, and 1h veto
block in that method. The remaining profile/consolidation logic consumes the
assessment's effective context; it must not reread the raw 1h decision as an
independent veto.

## Immutable signal evidence

Migration `V107` adds:

```sql
ALTER TABLE trade_signal ADD COLUMN data_quality_valid BOOLEAN NULL;
```

`AnalysisService` writes `MarketContextSnapshot.dataValid()` through the
existing `TradeSignal.builder()` call for every newly generated signal on every
interval. Historic rows are never backfilled: `NULL` means unknown/invalid.

FIX-154 does not depend on `signal_mode` or any other FIX-153 runtime/schema
feature. `data_quality_valid = true` is sufficient evidence eligibility for
this release; false or `NULL` is unusable. Historical-only exclusion is added
later with FIX-153, after its own migration/runtime release is approved.

Historical `btcContextEntryAllowed=false` is audit evidence only. It is never a
FIX-154 usability or hard-veto predicate. The current 1m BTC guard is unchanged.

## Time-bounded selection and fixed freshness

For a request with reference signal `R`, use `R.generatedAt` as `evaluatedAt`.
Select the latest same-symbol 5m and 1h signal such that both its generated
time and its candle close are at or before `evaluatedAt`; no wall-clock lookup
is permitted. Replay supplies the same historical reference time.

Existing fixed freshness limits remain authoritative:

| Interval | Maximum age |
|---|---:|
| 5m | 20 minutes |
| 1h | 3 hours |

`MISSING_5M_CONFIRMATION` and `MISSING_1H_CONTEXT` retain their existing
meaning and priority when their respective interval is missing or stale. A
stale signal is not quietly converted into an allowed BUY just because it is
`NOT_TRUSTED` for FIX-154.

## Usability and 1h strength

A fresh row is usable for FIX-154 only if `data_quality_valid = true`.

FIX-154 v1 does not invent ADX/DMI/VWAP/CMF evidence, because those indicators
are not currently persisted. Its fixed strength definition is:

```text
HARD_BEARISH_CANDIDATE = 1h decision == STRONG_SELL
                         AND confidence_score >= 70
SOFT_CONFLICT          = fresh, quality-valid 1h decision == SELL
                         OR STRONG_SELL with confidence_score below 70
NOT_TRUSTED            = fresh but quality-invalid/unknown/historical-only 1h
                         OR fresh non-bearish 1h
```

`confidence_score` is the persisted effective confidence. `raw_confidence_score`
is explicitly not used. The constant `70` is a named FIX-154 configuration
default, persisted in the audit row, and may change only through a reviewed
strategy change with Replay regression.

## Exact 1h A- 5m decision matrix

First apply existing missing/stale checks. Only then apply this table.

| 1h state | 5m state | Final HTF result before profile | Profile inputs |
|---|---|---|---|
| missing/stale | any | existing `MISSING_1H_CONTEXT` rejection | none |
| any | missing/stale | existing `MISSING_5M_CONFIRMATION` rejection | none |
| NOT_TRUSTED bearish from false/NULL quality | bearish | legacy `5M_BEARISH_VETO` | none |
| NOT_TRUSTED bearish from false/NULL quality | bullish/neutral/watch | `1H_BEARISH_VETO` | none |
| NOT_TRUSTED non-bearish from false/NULL quality | bearish | legacy `5M_BEARISH_VETO` | none |
| NOT_TRUSTED non-bearish from false/NULL quality | bullish/neutral/watch | continue to profile | raw 1h, raw 5m |
| SOFT_CONFLICT | bearish | legacy `5M_BEARISH_VETO` | none |
| SOFT_CONFLICT | bullish/neutral/watch | continue to profile, cap resulting first entry at 25% | effective 1h=`NEUTRAL`, raw 5m |
| HARD_BEARISH_CANDIDATE | bearish, quality-valid | legacy `5M_BEARISH_VETO`; audit `HARD_CONFIRMATION_PRESENT` | none |
| HARD_BEARISH_CANDIDATE | bullish/neutral/watch, quality-valid | `HTF_TRUSTED_BEARISH_VETO` | none |
| fresh, quality-valid non-bearish 1h | non-bearish | continue to profile | raw 1h, raw 5m |

The profile call produces the final decision for every `continue to profile`
row. It may still return its existing insufficient-confirmation code. It must
not consult the raw 1h row after this mapping.

`5m quality=false/NULL` does not alter the legacy 5m fallback. A fresh raw
bearish 5m still returns `5M_BEARISH_VETO`, exactly as today. It merely cannot
act as new FIX-154 hard-veto confirmation or a soft-conflict release.

The veto order is fixed: a bearish 5m returns `5M_BEARISH_VETO` before any
trusted-1h result. If the 1h is hard, audit records `HARD_CONFIRMATION_PRESENT`
on that legacy outcome. When 5m is not bearish, a hard trusted 1h returns
`HTF_TRUSTED_BEARISH_VETO`. The only deliberate lasting relaxation is a trusted
SOFT bearish 1h: an otherwise allowed initial entry is capped at 25%.

## Profile contract

`conservativeBuy`, `balancedBuy`, `balancedBuyWithNeutralFiveException`, and
`aggressiveBuy` receive the assessment's effective 1h/5m context’'?"not raw
selected 1h evidence. A SOFT conflict maps to `NEUTRAL`, never `WATCH`, then
the validation result is capped with `min(profilePositionPercent, 25)`.
This means the neutral-5m/direct-BUY exception still requires a genuinely
bullish 1h and cannot be unlocked by SOFT evidence. An untrusted non-bearish
1h retains its raw decision so deployment does not accidentally tighten current
bullish authority.

The assessment result carries `maxInitialPercent`: `25` for `SOFT_CONFLICT`,
otherwise no cap. Every caller that selects a size must use
`min(itsCalculatedPercent, maxInitialPercent)` when the cap is present. This
includes direct validation, `validateBuyContext()` callers (the 5m-trigger
path and accumulated evidence), and
`OpportunityConsolidationService.consolidatedPositionPercent()`.

The raw selected signals remain audit evidence. Existing 5m requirements inside
profiles remain unchanged. HARD and untrusted-bearish outcomes never call a
profile method.

### Add-on contract

`ExecutionIntelligenceService.progressivePositionDecision()` already reads raw
1h evidence and requires `WATCH` or bullish support for `SCOUT_ENTRY`,
`CONFIRMATION_ADD`, and `PROGRESSIVE_TREND_ADD`. Keep that check unchanged and
outside the three replacement paths; it already blocks HARD, SOFT, and
untrusted-bearish 1h states. Do not route it through effective FIX-154 context,
which could widen behavior. When that existing check rejects while the assessed
band is SOFT, record `SOFT_HTF_ADD_BLOCKED` as audit/metric evidence only.

## Append-only execution audit

FIX-154 is evaluated during execution/consolidation, not while a signal is
saved. Migration `V107` creates append-only `buy_htf_assessment_audit`:

- primary key ID;
- reference signal ID, evaluation path (`DIRECT_BUY`, `BUY_CONTEXT`,
  `CONSOLIDATION`, `PROGRESSIVE_TREND_ADD`, `CONFIRMATION_ADD`,
  `ACCUMULATED_EVIDENCE_ADD`) and `evaluated_at`; no unique constraint;
- raw/effective 1h and 5m signal IDs, decisions, signal modes, candle closes,
  ages, data-quality values, and usability reasons;
- configured confidence threshold, band, legacy-veto result, profile context,
  final result/reason, Production/Replay mode, and creation timestamp.

The audit is written best-effort after the relevant execution/consolidation
decision is known. A duplicate or persistence failure is logged and increments
`fix154.audit_write_failure_count`; it must never roll back, reject, or alter a
trading decision. It is evidence only’'?"not a fence or an input to later trading.

## Deployment behavior

Immediately after deployment all pre-V107 1h/5m rows have
`data_quality_valid=NULL`. They are treated as `NOT_TRUSTED`; they cannot make a
new permissive decision: a bearish unknown 1h remains blocked, while a
non-bearish unknown 1h retains its existing profile authority. The health view
must expose `fix154.untrusted_bearish_1h_block_count` and
`fix154.audit_write_failure_count`, `fix154.soft_capped_entry_count`, and
`fix154.soft_add_blocked_count`, grouped by execution path.

## Migration ordering and FIX-153 independence

The configured local database has V106 as its latest applied Flyway migration.
Therefore FIX-154 reserves `V107__fix_154_trusted_higher_timeframe_buy.sql`.
Before this migration is applied anywhere, rename the currently unreleased
`V107__fix_153_local_candle_reconciliation.sql` to V108. Never rename or edit
an applied migration. Do not enable Flyway `outOfOrder`; deployment order is
made explicit instead.

## Required regression suite

- All three integration paths use the shared service and no longer contain raw
  `1H_BEARISH_VETO` branches.
- `STRONG_SELL` + `confidence_score >= 70` and bearish fresh valid 5m produces
  legacy `5M_BEARISH_VETO` with `HARD_CONFIRMATION_PRESENT` audit; the same 1h
  with non-bearish 5m produces `HTF_TRUSTED_BEARISH_VETO`.
- Strong/soft/untrusted 1h plus bullish, neutral, watch, bearish, missing,
  stale, false-quality, and NULL-quality 5m covers every matrix row and its
  final profile result.
- `MISSING_1H_CONTEXT` (3h) and `MISSING_5M_CONFIRMATION` (20m) retain their
  priority and codes.
- BNB #150a: persisted historical 1h bearish row with `NULL` quality =>
  `NOT_TRUSTED` and `1H_BEARISH_VETO`.
- BNB #150b: Replay regenerates that same 1h with quality evidence and it is
  SOFT; no trusted hard veto is emitted and the final expected result is
  `REJECTED_RISK_REWARD` when the risk/reward guard fails.
- Historical `btcContextEntryAllowed=false` does not change usability; current
  1m BTC behavior is unchanged.
- FIX-154 migration succeeds with no `signal_mode` column and its assessment
  has no FIX-153 schema/runtime dependency.
- No future signal/candle is selected in Production or Replay; both produce
  equivalent audit evidence for the same historical inputs.
- Pre-migration `NULL` quality is never treated as valid.
- Repeated execution/consolidation evaluations append audit rows and audit
  failure leaves the business decision intact.
- A SOFT initial entry never exceeds 25% through progressive, confirmation, or
  accumulated-evidence or consolidation sizing; existing progressive raw-1h
  support rules block add stages while the 1h remains bearish.

## Release conditions

Run Java 21 unit/integration tests, migration tests, Production/Replay parity,
and a bounded no-look-ahead historical replay before enabling FIX-154.
FIX-153 remains independent and retains its own release gates.

## Revision addendum ’'?" Round 5 approval conditions

This addendum governs where it differs from earlier wording.

### Release build decision

The FIX-154 release build includes `V108__fix_153_local_candle_reconciliation.sql`
after the current unreleased FIX-153 V107 migration is renumbered to V108.
This is required even with reconciliation disabled: `candle_analysis_outcome`
is used by ordinary live signal processing, terminal worker outcomes, and the
closed-candle detector. Omitting V108 would cause missing-table SQL failures;
in particular, a normal signal-processing completion would roll back because
its outcome write is inside the processing transaction.

Including V108 ships FIX-153 reconciliation *schema only*. The reconciliation
runtime remains disabled and retains all of its separate release gates.

### No FIX-153 provenance dependency

Ignore earlier references to historical-only status or signal modes in the
FIX-154 band definition and audit payload. FIX-154 v1 eligibility depends only
on time-bounded selection and `data_quality_valid = true`; `signal_mode` is not
read, required, or stored by the FIX-154 audit.

### Effective context is transient

The assessment returns immutable effective decision values separately from the
loaded `TradeSignal` entities. No code may call `setDecision`, mutate, merge,
or save a selected 1h/5m signal to obtain effective `NEUTRAL` context. Profile
methods receive explicit effective decisions (or an immutable assessment
context), while the raw entity remains audit evidence only. A regression test
must load a SOFT 1h signal, execute the assessment/profile path, flush/clear,
and prove its stored decision is unchanged.

### Progressive-path evidence is observational

`progressivePositionDecision()` retains its existing raw-1h support check as
the only decision authority for scout/add paths. If that existing check passes,
FIX-154 performs no assessment lookup and writes no progressive audit row. If
it rejects because raw 1h is bearish, a best-effort audit-only assessment may
record `SOFT_HTF_ADD_BLOCKED` when the band is SOFT. That observation must not
alter the rejection, allow an add, cause a database lookup on passing paths, or
propagate an audit failure into the trading decision.

V108 ships with FIX-154 because the current runtime writes
`candle_analysis_outcome`. Reconciliation remains disabled.

### Round 5 text clarification

This addendum removes any earlier contrary implication: FIX-154 v1 has no
`signal_mode` or historical-only predicate or audit field. Effective 1h/5m
decisions are separate immutable values and never mutate a loaded signal.
Progressive-path assessment is audit-only after its existing raw-1h check has
already rejected; it is skipped entirely when that check passes and never feeds
the scout/add decision.

## Implementation completion record ’'?" 2026-10-10

The approved FIX-154 design is now implemented in the working tree.

- `V107__fix_154_trusted_higher_timeframe_buy.sql` creates the nullable signal
  quality evidence and append-only assessment audit table. No historical value
  is fabricated or backfilled.
- `AnalysisService` persists `MarketContextSnapshot.dataValid()` for every new
  signal.
- `HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentService` owns the hard/soft/untrusted decision
  matrix. It returns immutable effective decisions and does not mutate a JPA
  signal.
- Direct BUY validation, accumulated-evidence authority, and opportunity
  consolidation invoke that one service. A SOFT result uses effective 1h
  `NEUTRAL` and applies the 25% initial-position cap. The legacy bearish-5m
  veto retains precedence. Progressive adds retain their existing raw 1h
  requirement and can only create best-effort audit evidence after rejection.
- `BuyHtfAssessmentAuditStore` is append-only and runs in an independent
  transaction. It catches insert failures so audit availability cannot alter a
  business decision.

Focused Java 21 verification passed:
`HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentServiceTest`,
`TradeExecutionValidationServiceTest`, and
`OpportunityConsolidationServiceTest`.

This is implementation completion, not production deployment approval. Apply
V107/V108 only through the normal clean Java 21 release pipeline and complete
the required migration, Replay-parity, and broader regression validation before
enabling it in Production.

package com.crypto.domain;

import jakarta.persistence.*;
import lombok.*;

import java.math.BigDecimal;
import java.time.Instant;

@Entity
@Table(name = "paper_position")
@Getter @Setter @Builder @NoArgsConstructor @AllArgsConstructor
public class PaperPosition {
    @Id @GeneratedValue(strategy = GenerationType.IDENTITY)
    private Long id;

    @Column(nullable = false, length = 30)
    private String symbol;

    @Enumerated(EnumType.STRING)
    @Column(nullable = false, length = 10)
    private PositionSide side;

    @Enumerated(EnumType.STRING)
    @Column(nullable = false, length = 20)
    private PositionStatus status;

    @Column(nullable = false, precision = 30, scale = 12)
    private BigDecimal quantity;

    @Column(name = "entry_price", nullable = false, precision = 30, scale = 12)
    private BigDecimal entryPrice;

    @Column(name = "stop_loss", nullable = false, precision = 30, scale = 12)
    private BigDecimal stopLoss;

    @Column(name = "take_profit", nullable = false, precision = 30, scale = 12)
    private BigDecimal takeProfit;

    @Column(name = "exit_price", precision = 30, scale = 12)
    private BigDecimal exitPrice;

    @Column(name = "realized_pnl", precision = 30, scale = 12)
    private BigDecimal realizedPnl;

    @ManyToOne(fetch = FetchType.LAZY, optional = false)
    @JoinColumn(name = "signal_id")
    private TradeSignal signal;

    @ManyToOne(fetch = FetchType.LAZY)
    @JoinColumn(name = "exit_signal_id")
    private TradeSignal exitSignal;

    @Column(name = "close_reason", length = 40)
    private String closeReason;

    // FIX-144: Full signal/execution explanations and progressive additions exceed 2,000 chars.
    // Preserve audit text without truncation; V100 widens the existing physical columns.
    @Column(name = "entry_reason", columnDefinition = "MEDIUMTEXT")
    private String entryReason;

    // FIX-144: Exit audit explanations use the same expanded storage contract.
    @Column(name = "exit_reason", columnDefinition = "MEDIUMTEXT")
    private String exitReason;

    @Column(name = "opened_at", nullable = false)
    private Instant openedAt;

    @Column(name = "closed_at")
    private Instant closedAt;
}

package com.crypto.position.service;

import com.crypto.audit.service.ProductionExitAuditService;
import com.crypto.position.domain.PositionManagementEvent;
import com.crypto.position.repository.PositionManagementEventRepository;

import com.crypto.domain.PaperPosition;
import com.crypto.domain.PositionStatus;
import com.crypto.repository.PaperPositionRepository;
import com.crypto.repository.TradeSignalRepository;
import com.crypto.domain.TradeSignal;
import com.crypto.wallet.domain.WalletManagedPosition;
import com.crypto.wallet.repository.WalletManagedPositionRepository;
import com.crypto.wallet.service.WalletAutoExecutionService;
import lombok.RequiredArgsConstructor;
import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Service;
import org.springframework.transaction.annotation.Transactional;

import java.math.BigDecimal;
import java.math.MathContext;
import java.time.Instant;
import java.util.Locale;

/**
 * Mechanical protection for an already-open position using live Binance prices.
 * TP / SL / Profit Lock must not wait for the next analysis signal.
 */
@Service
@RequiredArgsConstructor
@Slf4j
public class LivePositionProtectionService {
    // FIX-125 mandatory in Spring; null only in existing constructor-only policy tests.
    @org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired
    private com.crypto.wallet.service.WalletTransactionCoordination walletCoordination;

    private static final MathContext MC = MathContext.DECIMAL64;

    private final WalletManagedPositionRepository managedPositionRepository;
    private final PaperPositionRepository paperPositionRepository;
    private final DynamicProfitLockService dynamicProfitLockService;
    private final PositionContinuationPolicy continuationPolicy;
    private final PositionExitPolicy exitPolicy;
    private final TradeSignalRepository tradeSignalRepository;
    private final WalletAutoExecutionService walletAutoExecutionService;
    private final ProductionExitAuditService productionExitAuditService;
    private final PositionManagementEventRepository positionManagementEventRepository;
    private final NearTpFailureProtectionPolicy nearTpFailureProtectionPolicy;

    // FIX-153: direct observations use atomic effects+outcome; shared callers retain their ledger API.
    @org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired private com.crypto.market.service.MarketPriceEventService priceEvents;
    @org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired(required=false) private com.crypto.local.ExchangeClockSynchronizer exchangeClock;
    @Transactional(isolation=org.springframework.transaction.annotation.Isolation.READ_COMMITTED)
    public void onPrice(String symbol,BigDecimal price,Instant observedAt,long eventId){
        price=com.crypto.market.service.MarketPriceEventService.normalizePrice(price);
        if(walletCoordination!=null)walletCoordination.symbol(symbol);
        // Acquire the existing managed-position lock BEFORE freshness, including waits on it.
        try {managedPositionRepository.findFirstBySymbolAndStatusOrderByOpenedAtDesc(symbol,"OPEN");}
        catch(RuntimeException failure){
            // FIX-153: preserve only the established pre-business position-deadlock retry permission.
            if(com.crypto.infrastructure.transaction.InitialPositionLockDeadlock.isMysqlDeadlock(failure))
                throw new com.crypto.infrastructure.transaction.InitialPositionLockDeadlock(failure);
            throw failure;
        }
        Instant localNow=Instant.now();
        String rejection=priceEvents.invalidReason(price,observedAt,exchangeClock==null?localNow:exchangeClock.correctJvm(localNow));
        if(rejection==null && priceEvents.laterApplied(symbol,observedAt))rejection="OUT_OF_ORDER";
        if(rejection!=null){priceEvents.finish(eventId,symbol,observedAt,price,"NON_CANONICAL",rejection);return;}
        onPrice(symbol,price);
        // No-position and all successful no-op exits reach this statement; exceptions do not.
        priceEvents.finish(eventId,symbol,observedAt,price,"APPLIED","PROTECTION_CALLBACK_COMPLETED");
    }

    @Transactional
    public void onPrice(String symbolValue, BigDecimal price) {
        if (symbolValue == null || symbolValue.isBlank() || price == null || price.signum() <= 0) return;
        String symbol = symbolValue.trim().toUpperCase(Locale.ROOT);

        // FIX-124: classify ONLY the first lock failure. The caller may retry after
        // rollback in a fresh transaction; failures after evaluation are not retried.
        if(walletCoordination!=null)walletCoordination.symbol(symbol);
        WalletManagedPosition managed;
        try {
            managed = managedPositionRepository
                    .findFirstBySymbolAndStatusOrderByOpenedAtDesc(symbol, "OPEN")
                    .orElse(null);
        } catch (RuntimeException failure) {
            if (com.crypto.infrastructure.transaction.InitialPositionLockDeadlock.isMysqlDeadlock(failure)) {
                throw new com.crypto.infrastructure.transaction.InitialPositionLockDeadlock(failure);
            }
            throw failure;
        }
        if (managed == null || managed.getQuantity() == null || managed.getQuantity().signum() <= 0) return;

        TradeSignal one = tradeSignalRepository.findTopBySymbolAndIntervalOrderByGeneratedAtDesc(symbol, "1m").orElse(null);
        TradeSignal five = tradeSignalRepository.findTopBySymbolAndIntervalOrderByGeneratedAtDesc(symbol, "5m").orElse(null);
        TradeSignal hour = tradeSignalRepository.findTopBySymbolAndIntervalOrderByGeneratedAtDesc(symbol, "1h").orElse(null);

        // Reaching TP is a management checkpoint, not an unconditional full exit.
        if (managed.getTakeProfitUsdt() != null && price.compareTo(managed.getTakeProfitUsdt()) >= 0) {
            // Pass the complete immutable BUY thesis. PositionContinuationPolicy and
            // PositionManagementService now evaluate the same deterioration pressure (FIX-011).
            PositionContinuationPolicy.Evaluation continuation = continuationPolicy.evaluate(one, five, hour,
                    managed.getEntryTrendScore(), managed.getEntryStructureScore(),
                    managed.getEntryMomentumScore(), managed.getEntryVolumeScore(),
                    managed.getEntryConfidence(), managed.getEntryTotalScore());
            if (continuation.extendTarget()) {
                BigDecimal distance = managed.getTakeProfitUsdt().subtract(managed.getAverageEntryPriceUsdt());
                BigDecimal oldTarget = managed.getTakeProfitUsdt();
                BigDecimal newTarget = oldTarget.add(distance.multiply(BigDecimal.valueOf(0.50), MC), MC);
                Instant changedAt = Instant.now();

                // FIX-118 behavioral phase: if Profit Lock was already earned under the old TP
                // but the wider approved target moves current progress back below activation,
                // persist TP_EXTENSION_REBASE before saving the new geometry. The historical
                // lock remains stored but is non-executable until new-geometry activation is
                // earned again.
                DynamicProfitLockService.ExtensionTransition fix118Transition =
                        dynamicProfitLockService.onTakeProfitExtended(managed, newTarget, price, changedAt);
                log.info("[FIX-118][PRODUCTION][TP_EXTENSION] positionId={}, symbol={}, marketPrice={}, entry={}, oldTp={}, newTp={}, " +
                                "highest={}, profitLockActive={}, profitLockPrice={}, previousState={}, state={}, " +
                                "newGeometryProgressPct={}, activationPct={}",
                        managed.getId(), symbol, price, managed.getAverageEntryPriceUsdt(), oldTarget, newTarget,
                        managed.getHighestPriceUsdt(), managed.isProfitLockActive(), managed.getProfitLockPriceUsdt(),
                        fix118Transition.previousState(), fix118Transition.state(), fix118Transition.progressPercent(),
                        fix118Transition.activationPercent());

                managed.setTakeProfitUsdt(newTarget);
                // FIX-11T: TP extension changes the planned entry->TP distance. Any old Near-TP
                // arm/rejection was relative to the superseded target and must be restarted.
                resetNearTpTrackingForNewRiskGeometry(managed);
                managed.setUpdatedAt(changedAt);
                managedPositionRepository.save(managed);

                // FIX-067: Production has two persisted views of the same open lifecycle:
                // wallet_managed_position drives live protection, while paper_position is also
                // evaluated when a fresh TradeSignal arrives.  Before this fix only the managed
                // position received a TAKE_PROFIT_EXTENDED update.  The next 1m signal could
                // therefore see the stale ORIGINAL paper TP and close the wallet early.
                // PEPE wallet #833 / managed position #552 proved the race: managed TP was
                // extended 0.000004146897 -> 0.000004165346, but the signal path sold at
                // 0.000004150 because paper_position still held 0.000004146897.
                // Keep both Production state holders synchronized inside this same transaction.
                PaperPosition synchronizedPaper = paperPositionRepository
                        .findBySymbolAndStatus(symbol, PositionStatus.OPEN)
                        .orElse(null);
                if (synchronizedPaper != null) {
                    synchronizedPaper.setTakeProfit(newTarget);
                    paperPositionRepository.save(synchronizedPaper);
                }

                // FIX-053: Persist every TP revision so Dashboard can show the actual
                // Production management path instead of only the latest target value.
                positionManagementEventRepository.save(PositionManagementEvent.builder()
                        .walletPositionId(managed.getId())
                        .symbol(symbol)
                        .eventType("TAKE_PROFIT_EXTENDED")
                        .oldValueUsdt(oldTarget)
                        .newValueUsdt(newTarget)
                        .marketPriceUsdt(price)
                        .reason(continuation.explanation())
                        .occurredAt(changedAt)
                        .build());
                log.info("Live TAKE_PROFIT extended: symbol={}, oldTarget={}, newTarget={}, reason={}", symbol, oldTarget, newTarget, continuation.explanation());
            } else if (walletAutoExecutionService.executeMechanicalExit(
                    symbol, price, "TAKE_PROFIT", continuation.explanation())) {
                closePaper(symbol, price, PositionStatus.CLOSED, "TAKE_PROFIT", continuation.explanation(), one);
                log.info("Live TAKE_PROFIT executed: symbol={}, price={}, target={}", symbol, price, managed.getTakeProfitUsdt());
            }
            return;
        }

        // Stop loss is absolute and is never softened by continuation logic.
        if (managed.getStopLossUsdt() != null && price.compareTo(managed.getStopLossUsdt()) <= 0) {
            if (walletAutoExecutionService.executeMechanicalExit(
                    symbol, price, "STOP_LOSS",
                    "Live price " + price + " reached stop loss " + managed.getStopLossUsdt())) {
                closePaper(symbol, price, PositionStatus.STOPPED, "STOP_LOSS",
                        "Live market price reached the configured stop loss.", one);
                log.info("Live STOP_LOSS executed: symbol={}, price={}, stop={}", symbol, price, managed.getStopLossUsdt());
            }
            return;
        }

        DynamicProfitLockService.Evaluation lock = dynamicProfitLockService.evaluatePrice(symbol, price);

        // FIX-118: during TP_EXTENSION_REBASE the superseded lock is intentionally
        // non-executable, but earned profit is not left completely unprotected. Stop Loss
        // has already had absolute priority above; this standalone floor is the next safety
        // authority and does not depend on the old lock being triggered.
        if (lock.hardProfitFloorTriggered()) {
            String reason = lock.explanation();
            log.info("[FIX-118][PRODUCTION][REBASE_HARD_FLOOR] positionId={}, symbol={}, price={}, floor={}, target={}",
                    lock.walletPositionId(), symbol, price, lock.hardProfitFloor(), managed.getTakeProfitUsdt());
            if (walletAutoExecutionService.executeMechanicalExit(symbol, price, "PROFIT_LOCK_HARD_EXIT", reason)) {
                closePaper(symbol, price, PositionStatus.CLOSED, "PROFIT_LOCK_HARD_EXIT", reason, one);
            }
            return;
        }

        if (lock.active() || lock.triggered() || lock.rebasing()) {
            log.info("[FIX-118][PRODUCTION][LIVE_PATH] positionId={}, symbol={}, price={}, target={}, highest={}, active={}, " +
                            "progressPct={}, activationPct={}, lock={}, state={}, triggered={}",
                    lock.walletPositionId(), symbol, price, managed.getTakeProfitUsdt(), lock.highestPrice(), lock.active(),
                    lock.progressPercent(), lock.activationPercent(), lock.lockPrice(), lock.state(), lock.triggered());
        }
        if (lock.triggered()) {
            log.info("[FIX-118][PRODUCTION][LIVE_TRIGGER] positionId={}, symbol={}, price={}, lock={}, progressPct={}, target={}",
                    lock.walletPositionId(), symbol, price, lock.lockPrice(), lock.progressPercent(), managed.getTakeProfitUsdt());
            BigDecimal hardProfitFloor = managed.getAverageEntryPriceUsdt().multiply(BigDecimal.valueOf(1.0005), MC);
            if (price.compareTo(hardProfitFloor) < 0) {
                String reason = lock.explanation() + " Hard protected-profit floor " + hardProfitFloor + " was breached.";
                if (walletAutoExecutionService.executeMechanicalExit(symbol, price, "PROFIT_LOCK_HARD_EXIT", reason)) {
                    closePaper(symbol, price, PositionStatus.CLOSED, "PROFIT_LOCK_HARD_EXIT", reason, one);
                }
                return;
            }

            PositionExitPolicy.Evaluation lockDecision = exitPolicy.evaluateProfitLockBreach(one, five, hour);
            if (lockDecision.exit()) {
                String reason = lock.explanation() + " " + lockDecision.explanation();
                if (walletAutoExecutionService.executeMechanicalExit(symbol, price, lockDecision.code(), reason)) {
                    closePaper(symbol, price, PositionStatus.CLOSED, lockDecision.code(), reason, one);
                    log.info("Live PROFIT_LOCK exit: symbol={}, price={}, lock={}, reason={}",
                            symbol, price, lock.lockPrice(), lockDecision.code());
                }
                return;
            }
            log.info("Live PROFIT_LOCK protected hold: symbol={}, price={}, lock={}, reason={}",
                    symbol, price, lock.lockPrice(), lockDecision.explanation());
        }

        // Normal signal exits remain active even when Profit Lock is disabled. Higher-timeframe
        // failure can therefore close a winner instead of waiting forever for an exact 1m SELL event.
        PositionExitPolicy.Evaluation normalExit = exitPolicy.evaluateNormalExit(one, five, hour);
        if (normalExit.exit()) {
            if (walletAutoExecutionService.executeMechanicalExit(symbol, price, normalExit.code(), normalExit.explanation())) {
                closePaper(symbol, price, PositionStatus.CLOSED, normalExit.code(), normalExit.explanation(), one);
                log.info("Live signal-driven exit: symbol={}, price={}, reason={}", symbol, price, normalExit.code());
            }
            // FIX-11T: any higher-priority position action owns this evaluation cycle.
            // Near-TP must never race another SELL or quantity-changing decision on the same tick.
            return;
        }

        // FIX-11T: Near-TP Failure Protection is deliberately LAST. TP/SL/profit-lock and
        // existing signal exits above retain their exact authority and always win the cycle.
        evaluateNearTpProtection(managed, price, one, five);
    }

    private void evaluateNearTpProtection(WalletManagedPosition managed, BigDecimal price,
                                          TradeSignal oneMinute, TradeSignal fiveMinute) {
        // FIX-11T evidence is explicitly time-safe. Normal Production logic above keeps its
        // existing signal lookup unchanged; Near-TP alone falls back to an at-or-before query
        // if the repository's newest row is future-dated relative to this live evaluation.
        Instant evaluatedAt = Instant.now();
        oneMinute = latestEligibleSignal(managed.getSymbol(), "1m", oneMinute, evaluatedAt);
        fiveMinute = latestEligibleSignal(managed.getSymbol(), "5m", fiveMinute, evaluatedAt);

        NearTpFailureProtectionPolicy.State state = new NearTpFailureProtectionPolicy.State(
                managed.getNearTpState() == null ? NearTpState.INACTIVE : managed.getNearTpState(),
                managed.getNearTpBestPrice(), managed.getNearTpBearishStreak(),
                managed.getNearTpLastOneMinuteSignalId(), managed.isNearTpHarvestUsed());

        NearTpFailureProtectionPolicy.Evaluation evaluation = nearTpFailureProtectionPolicy.evaluate(
                state, managed.getAverageEntryPriceUsdt(), managed.getTakeProfitUsdt(),
                price, evaluatedAt, oneMinute, fiveMinute);

        NearTpFailureProtectionPolicy.State next = evaluation.state();
        boolean stateChanged = managed.getNearTpState() != next.nearTpState()
                || !sameDecimal(managed.getNearTpBestPrice(), next.bestPrice())
                || managed.getNearTpBearishStreak() != next.consecutiveBearishOneMinute()
                || !java.util.Objects.equals(managed.getNearTpLastOneMinuteSignalId(), next.lastEvaluatedOneMinuteSignalId());
        if (stateChanged) {
            managed.setNearTpState(next.nearTpState());
            managed.setNearTpBestPrice(next.bestPrice());
            managed.setNearTpBearishStreak(next.consecutiveBearishOneMinute());
            managed.setNearTpLastOneMinuteSignalId(next.lastEvaluatedOneMinuteSignalId());
            managed.setUpdatedAt(Instant.now());
            managedPositionRepository.save(managed);
        }

        if (evaluation.transition() || stateChanged) {
            log.info("FIX-11T Near-TP evaluation: positionId={}, symbol={}, state={}, code={}, price={}, bestPrice={}, " +
                            "tpProgressPct={}, givebackPct={}, bearish1mStreak={}, oneMinuteId={}, oneMinuteOriginal={}, " +
                            "fiveMinuteId={}, fiveMinuteOriginal={}, detail={}",
                    managed.getId(), managed.getSymbol(), next.nearTpState(), evaluation.code(), price, next.bestPrice(),
                    evaluation.tpProgressPercent(), evaluation.givebackPercent(), next.consecutiveBearishOneMinute(),
                    oneMinute == null ? null : oneMinute.getId(),
                    oneMinute == null ? null : oneMinute.getOriginalDecision(),
                    fiveMinute == null ? null : fiveMinute.getId(),
                    fiveMinute == null ? null : fiveMinute.getOriginalDecision(),
                    evaluation.explanation());
        } else if (log.isDebugEnabled() && evaluation.code().startsWith("HOLD_")) {
            log.debug("FIX-11T Near-TP hold: positionId={}, symbol={}, code={}, price={}, detail={}",
                    managed.getId(), managed.getSymbol(), evaluation.code(), price, evaluation.explanation());
        }

        if (!evaluation.harvestEligible()) return;

        WalletAutoExecutionService.PartialHarvestResult harvest = walletAutoExecutionService
                .executeNearTpPartialHarvest(managed.getSymbol(), price, evaluation.explanation());
        if (!harvest.executed()) {
            log.warn("FIX-11T Near-TP harvest not executed: positionId={}, symbol={}, status={}, price={}",
                    managed.getId(), managed.getSymbol(), harvest.status(), price);
            // If execution did not happen, return to rejection monitoring rather than leaving
            // the state stranded in FAILURE_CONFIRMED. Idempotency still lives in wallet_trade.
            managed.setNearTpState(NearTpState.NEAR_TP_REJECTION_DETECTED);
            if ("BELOW_BINANCE_MINIMUM".equals(harvest.status())
                    || "BINANCE_MINIMUM_UNAVAILABLE".equals(harvest.status())) {
                // The skipped order consumed the current confirmation pair. Requiring fresh
                // bearish evidence prevents a retry on every subsequent market-price tick.
                managed.setNearTpBearishStreak(0);
            }
            managed.setUpdatedAt(Instant.now());
            managedPositionRepository.save(managed);
            return;
        }

        // Keep paper_position quantity/P&L synchronized with the managed wallet lifecycle.
        // Without this, a later terminal signal close would calculate P&L on pre-harvest quantity.
        PaperPosition paper = paperPositionRepository.findBySymbolAndStatus(managed.getSymbol(), PositionStatus.OPEN).orElse(null);
        if (paper != null) {
            paper.setQuantity(harvest.remainingQuantity());
            BigDecimal priorRealized = paper.getRealizedPnl() == null ? BigDecimal.ZERO : paper.getRealizedPnl();
            paper.setRealizedPnl(priorRealized.add(harvest.realizedPnlUsdt(), MC));
            paperPositionRepository.save(paper);
        }

        log.info("FIX-11T NEAR_TP_PARTIAL_HARVESTED: positionId={}, symbol={}, soldQty={}, remainingQty={}, " +
                        "price={}, realizedPnl={}, realizedPnlPct={}",
                managed.getId(), managed.getSymbol(), harvest.soldQuantity(), harvest.remainingQuantity(),
                price, harvest.realizedPnlUsdt(), harvest.realizedPnlPercent());
    }

    private TradeSignal latestEligibleSignal(String symbol, String interval, TradeSignal newest, Instant evaluatedAt) {
        if (newest != null && newest.getGeneratedAt() != null && !newest.getGeneratedAt().isAfter(evaluatedAt)) {
            return newest;
        }
        return tradeSignalRepository
                .findTopBySymbolAndIntervalAndGeneratedAtLessThanEqualOrderByGeneratedAtDesc(symbol, interval, evaluatedAt)
                .orElse(null);
    }

    /**
     * FIX-117: TP extension must not erase Near-TP protection that was already earned.
     *
     * Only ARMED and REJECTION_DETECTED change from the pre-FIX-117 behavior. INACTIVE
     * still resets against the new geometry. FAILURE_CONFIRMED also keeps the existing
     * reset behavior because that persisted/transient lifecycle has a real interruption
     * window between state save and harvest resolution and is intentionally out of scope.
     * PARTIAL_HARVESTED remains terminal through the existing harvest-used guard.
     *
     * plannedDistance is recalculated from the current TP by NearTpFailureProtectionPolicy
     * on every evaluation, so the historical best price remains a valid market observation
     * and does not need synthetic rebasing after an extension.
     */
    private void resetNearTpTrackingForNewRiskGeometry(WalletManagedPosition managed) {
        if (managed.isNearTpHarvestUsed()) return;

        NearTpState currentState = managed.getNearTpState() == null
                ? NearTpState.INACTIVE : managed.getNearTpState();
        switch (currentState) {
            case INACTIVE, NEAR_TP_FAILURE_CONFIRMED -> {
                // Existing behavior: no earned protection is carried into the new geometry.
                managed.setNearTpState(NearTpState.INACTIVE);
                managed.setNearTpBestPrice(null);
                managed.setNearTpBearishStreak(0);
                managed.setNearTpLastOneMinuteSignalId(null);
            }
            case NEAR_TP_ARMED -> {
                // FIX-117: preserve earned protection and the real historical best price.
            }
            case NEAR_TP_REJECTION_DETECTED -> {
                // Preserve earned protection/best price, but discard bearish persistence
                // accumulated under the old, tighter geometry. The next evaluation will
                // recalculate giveback against the new TP and either recover or remain rejected.
                managed.setNearTpBearishStreak(0);
                managed.setNearTpLastOneMinuteSignalId(null);
            }
            case NEAR_TP_PARTIAL_HARVESTED -> {
                // Existing terminal behavior; normally returned above by nearTpHarvestUsed.
            }
        }
    }

    private boolean sameDecimal(BigDecimal a, BigDecimal b) {
        return a == null ? b == null : b != null && a.compareTo(b) == 0;
    }

    private void closePaper(String symbol, BigDecimal exitPrice, PositionStatus status,
                            String closeReason, String explanation, TradeSignal sourceSignal) {
        PaperPosition paper = paperPositionRepository.findBySymbolAndStatus(symbol, PositionStatus.OPEN).orElse(null);
        if (paper == null) return;
        BigDecimal remainingPnl = exitPrice.subtract(paper.getEntryPrice(), MC).multiply(paper.getQuantity(), MC);
        // FIX-11T: paper_position may already contain realized P&L from a Near-TP partial
        // harvest. Preserve it when the remaining quantity is later closed.
        BigDecimal pnl = (paper.getRealizedPnl() == null ? BigDecimal.ZERO : paper.getRealizedPnl())
                .add(remainingPnl, MC);
        paper.setExitPrice(exitPrice);
        paper.setRealizedPnl(pnl);
        paper.setStatus(status);
        paper.setCloseReason(closeReason);
        paper.setExitReason(explanation);
        paper.setClosedAt(Instant.now());
        PaperPosition saved = paperPositionRepository.save(paper);

        // FIX-028: live-price exits (TP/SL/Profit Lock/normal exit) also write the
        // immutable audit record. sourceSignal is context only unless the closeTrigger
        // itself is a genuine SELL decision. No protection/exit policy is changed here.
        productionExitAuditService.record(saved, sourceSignal, closeReason, explanation);
    }
}

## AGENTS.md Protection

This file contains permanent project instructions.

Codex MUST NOT modify AGENTS.md unless the user explicitly requests
or approves the modification.

Codex may automatically maintain:
- codex-docs/ARCHITECTURE.md
- codex-docs/CHANGELOG_AI.md
- codex-docs/TRADING_RULES.md
- codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md

## Mandatory Documentation Maintenance

Documentation updates are part of the implementation and are NOT optional.

After every implementation, Codex MUST determine whether the change affects
architecture, trading behavior, operational behavior, or previous technical
decisions.

### Architecture changes
If the implementation changes any of the following:
- component responsibilities
- data flow
- database/table ownership
- event flow
- WebSocket usage
- service dependencies
- application boundaries
- ingestion architecture
- Replay/Production architecture

Codex MUST update:

docs/ARCHITECTURE.md

The architecture documentation must describe the FINAL implemented state,
not only what was originally planned.

### Every completed fix/change
Codex MUST update:

docs/CHANGELOG_AI.md

Include:
- date
- problem
- root cause
- solution
- files/classes changed
- database impact
- Production impact
- Replay impact

### Trading behavior changes
Codex MUST update:

docs/TRADING_RULES.md

### Investigation findings
Important investigation findings and rejected approaches MUST be recorded in:

docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md

## Definition of Done

A task is NOT COMPLETE until all required documentation has been updated.

Before finishing a task, Codex MUST explicitly check:
"Does this change require documentation updates?"

Do not wait for the user to ask for documentation updates.


## Trade investigations
When asked to investigate a trade:

- Confirm the application, environment, symbol, and trade ID.
- Use configured database and SSH connections without displaying secrets.
- Use read-only, bounded queries and targeted log searches.
- Read the actual schema before choosing tables or columns.
- Reconstruct the timeline: candle, signal, decision, entry, protection,
  and exit. Display timestamps in KSA (UTC+03:00), interpreting stored
  timestamps as UTC.
- Trace the relevant code and configuration for each decision.
- Check data freshness, entry quality, strategy, risk/reward, fees,
  stop-loss, and exit reason where applicable.
- Assess the entry using only information available at that moment.
  Use subsequent price action separately to evaluate the outcome.
- Distinguish a valid losing trade from a logic defect, stale data,
  execution delay, configuration issue, or missing evidence.
- Check whether the behavior can be reproduced in Replay.
- Support findings with record IDs, log timestamps, and code references.
- State uncertainties; do not invent a cause.
- Do not change code, configuration, services, or production data during
  an investigation unless the user requests a fix.

Report:
1. What happened.
2. Why the system entered and exited.
3. Whether the behavior followed the intended rules.
4. Confirmed defects and missing evidence.
5. Recommended correction and regression checks, if needed.

## Current architecture direction (pending implementation)

The current priority is to remove the trader's runtime dependency on the
collector. This is a design decision, not an implemented/deployed change.

- Target runtime: trader-owned Binance kline ingestion and local
  `crypto_ai.candle` writes with `shared-market.mode=OFF`.
- The collector remains independent in `crypto_ai_v2`; the trader must not
  connect to, read from, or write collector stream/cursor tables at runtime.
- Do not run direct ingestion and the shared-market executors together.
- Applied shared-market tables and Flyway history are historical evidence; do
  not drop, reset, or edit them merely to enable the new path.
- `V103__fix_149_fresh_admission_indexes` still requires
  `crypto_ai_v2.market_data_stream_event` for a fresh Flyway install. Treat
  trader-only bootstrap as a release blocker until a forward, tested installer
  policy exists. Never edit an applied Flyway migration.

## Local-candle implementation safety

When implementing or reviewing the local-candle design:

- Keep `shared-market.fix142.enabled=false`,
  `shared-market.fix142.acknowledgment-enabled=false`, and scheduled analysis
  disabled for the OFF-mode cutover unless a reviewed change explicitly says
  otherwise.
- Preserve the input -> protection -> observer -> close-dispatch transaction
  order. Candle close analysis must not run on the Binance ingestion thread.
- A closed candle needs durable/recoverable analysis admission; an in-memory
  event/queue alone loses work across a crash. Recovery must distinguish fresh
  execution-capable work from historical-only analysis.
- Candle updates must be monotonic: a late open update cannot reopen or
  overwrite a closed candle. Publish close work only for an atomic
  open-to-closed transition.
- A WebSocket reconnect can occur at a candle-close boundary and Binance may
  not replay that final closed-kline event. Candle recovery is automatic: use
  an exact authoritative Binance REST candle only after exchange-time
  finality and the measured live-close grace. It may either finalise a matching
  existing `closed=0` row or insert a wholly absent identity inside the active
  data-quality window. A recovered open row preserves provenance; an inserted
  gap row uses `BACKFILL` provenance. Neither path may alter a completed row,
  create a close-transition token, dispatch analysis, create a live signal,
  or execute a trade. The recovery must have durable retry/audit evidence,
  bounded rate-limit handling, health visibility, and no manual repair step.
- Automation direction: no pending candle or analysis outcome may require
  manual approval, manual database repair, or a persisted trading fence.
  Reconciliation must retry and resolve automatically, or age out to a durable
  explicit outcome with a visible reason. This does not permit unverified data
  to masquerade as valid or force an entry: a genuine strategy/risk veto or
  unavailable authoritative data remains non-executable evidence, not a
  silently stuck pending signal.
- Gap backfill must be a pure insert-if-absent operation. It must never use a
  duplicate-key update that could finalise or alter a live row which appeared
  while the authoritative REST request was in progress. Recovery candidates
  are bounded by the active quality window and terminal audit outcomes are not
  retried. Each candle/work path must write one durable terminal analysis
  outcome; transient failures remain retryable and write no terminal outcome.
- Any future universal trading-mutation barrier is an in-memory drain
  participant only. Outside an active drain it must not block, delay, queue,
  or persistently fence fresh trading, signals, or protection. It may reject
  new mutations only while draining and must then wait for admitted mutations
  to finish before shutdown.
- Historical-only recovery must be structurally unable to reach execution,
  wallet, position, order, or executable-work services. Quarantined work with
  possible financial effects may be concluded automatically only from proven
  durable effect evidence (`EFFECT_COMMITTED` or `EFFECT_ABSENT`); unknown
  atomicity remains a release blocker. Binance REST rate-limit/ban handling
  must be shared by reconciliation, clock sync, bootstrap, and other REST
  callers.
- Enforce freshness from persisted candle close time before analysis and again
  before execution. Historical-only signals must be explicitly marked and
  excluded from live-latency monitoring.
- Price protection must receive the Binance observation timestamp and validate
  age/order inside the protection transaction. Paper-trading effects and their
  accepted price-event state must commit atomically; any future live-exchange
  effect needs a separate idempotent design.
- Do not add manual approval, persisted review fences, or database locks that
  block new analysis, signals, entries, or protection unless the user changes
  that direction. In-process coordination and normal database constraints are
  distinct from persisted trading fences.
- OFF-mode health/admin endpoints must not query or write retired shared-market
  tables. Existing `REVIEW_REQUIRED` history must not block OFF-mode work.
- Keep production and Replay evidence aligned. Replay must fail a parity claim
  for unresolved direct price-event evidence, while deliberate exclusions stay
  excluded.

## Delivery discipline

- Treat the local-candle architecture as not deployment-ready until its
  migration/bootstrap policy, concurrency, crash/restart, stale-input,
  Replay-parity, and Java 21/MySQL validation gates pass.
- Do not deploy or change production configuration/data unless the user
  explicitly requests it.
- Protection coverage for open positions during application replacement remains
  an unresolved release gate. Do not represent the local-candle design as
  deployment-ready until it is implemented or conclusively disproven.

## FIX-154: pending higher-timeframe BUY validation

FIX-154 is a separate, not-yet-implemented design. Do not merge it into the
local-candle change or describe it as deployed.

Purpose: a historical bearish 1h signal may block or delay a current 1m BUY
only when its own evidence is fresh, data-quality-valid, and sufficiently
strong. A historical `btcContextEntryAllowed=false` must remain investigatory
evidence, not a higher-timeframe validity gate; the current 1m signal's own
BTC protection remains unchanged.

- Score usable bearish 1h evidence into `NOT_TRUSTED`, `SOFT_CONFLICT`, or
  `HARD_BEARISH_CANDIDATE` using closed-candle local indicators.
- A hard veto requires strong 1h evidence plus fresh, quality-valid bearish
  5m confirmation. Stale, invalid, or unknown historical quality can never
  hard-veto.
- Persist `trade_signal.data_quality_valid` from
  `MarketContextSnapshot.dataValid()` for every new signal. Historical NULL
  means unknown/invalid; never backfill it to true.
- A confirmed 1h recovery may release only a soft conflict and only with fresh,
  quality-valid, non-bearish 5m evidence.
- Preserve the legacy `5M_BEARISH_VETO` behavior: the new data-quality gate
  applies to the new hard-veto/recovery paths, not that legacy fallback.
- Add immutable BUY-evaluation audit evidence; maintain Replay parity and
  prevent future-candle or wall-clock look-ahead.

This repository uses persistent AI engineering knowledge.

No agent may begin a technical investigation, architecture analysis,
trade investigation, solution design, or implementation without first
loading the required project context.

2. agent-docs/AGREED_KNOWLEDGE.md
3. agent-docs/DECISIONS.md

These contain shared team knowledge and approved decisions.

notes when they conflict, unless current implementation evidence proves the
shared knowledge is outdated.

reason.
